PREFACE.

## *Chapter XIX. On the use of audible things in the casting out of the Devil, and on their order in the following work.*

<margin>The Devil is put to flight by audible sounds.</margin>
Both the sacred canons and the canonical scriptures testify that audible sounds contribute greatly to the expulsion of the Devil from possessed bodies. For who has ever recognized even the meaning of the name "Exorcist," unless they oppose this proposition with a stubborn will? <margin>What the name "Exorcist" signifies.</margin> For this word "Exorcist," being a transposition from the Greek into Latin, means the same as "one who adjures" or "one who rebukes." <note>original: "adjurans, sive increpans." In this context, to adjure is to command a spirit solemnly as if under oath.</note> This is stated in the Chapter *Cleros*, near the end of the 21st Distinction. <note>This refers to the *Decretum Gratiani*, a 12th-century collection of Canon Law that was a standard legal text for the Church.</note> And does not a rebuke relate to the sense of hearing? We have learned this same truth from the First Book of Kings, chapter 16: <note>In the Latin Vulgate Bible used by the author, 1 & 2 Kings correspond to 1 & 2 Samuel in modern English Bibles. This refers to David soothing King Saul.</note> when we knew that the evil spirit from the Lord departed from King Saul at the harmonic sound of the lyre, played with David's wondrous skill. Therefore, in making a proposal regarding audible things in the present work, I suggest two types: the first pertains to **sound**; the second to **the voice**.

<margin>When the bell and organs are to be used.</margin>
As for what pertains to sound, it is twofold for our purposes: first is the **small bell**, <note>original: "Campanella." Small hand-bells were often used in liturgy to signal transitions or to command the attention of both the congregation and the spiritual world.</note> whose sound shall be raised during the stirrings, the citations [of the spirit], and the fixing of boundaries. Second are the **organs**, the playing of which should be put to use during the singing of the Psalms in alternating turns after the manner of choir singers, and whenever the mind of the possessed person must be refreshed or the Devil must be struck. 

As for the second type, which pertains to the voice, these are the **Exorcisms**; and whatever must be either spoken or read during the process of exorcising. All these things in this following work of mine are organized under a threefold series. Those in the first are called **Pre-exorcisms**; those in the second, **Exorcisms**; and those in the third, **Post-exorcisms**. 

The Pre-exorcisms are five: the first is the general confession of the Exorcist; the second is the general confession of the possessed person and their absolution; the third is the invocation to be offered to God; the fourth is the protestation <note>original: "protestatio." A formal declaration or warning delivered to the demons to establish the Exorcist's authority.</note> to be made known to the demons; the fifth is the stirring up of the evil

->PREFACE.<-

...spirits existing within the possessed person; The sixth is the oath to be offered to the Demon; <note>original: "juramentum Daemoni præbendum." This likely refers to a ritualized demand where the exorcist forces the demon to swear to tell the truth or to depart.</note> And the seventh is the order for requesting information from the Demons. 

The **Exorcisms** themselves are nine: The first is from the holy names of God; The second is from the order of all the Saints; The third is from the noteworthy chief Angels; The fourth is from the acts of the life of the glorious Virgin Mary; The fifth is from the deeds of our Lord Jesus Christ; The sixth is from the institutions of the Venerable Sacraments; The seventh is from the chief Dogmas of the Holy Church; The eighth is from the mysteries of the Most Holy Trinity and the unity of God; and the ninth is from the book of the Apocalypse of the blessed John the Apostle. <note>The Book of Revelation.</note>

After the exorcisms, the **Post-exorcisms** <term>Post-exorcisms: follow-up rites intended to seal the healing or address lingering effects after the primary spirit has been commanded to leave.</term> are reduced to two types: The first regards the internal bodily invasion of the possessed person to some degree; and the second pertains to the sensible external harassment of men. 

Under the first type, there are five **Post-exorcisms**: 
The first is the Command to be made to the spirits who have departed; 
The second is the command to be made to the evil spirits remaining in the body; 
The third is the curse against overly obstinate Demons; 
The fourth is the giving of thanks for the liberated person; 
And the fifth is the blessing of that same person. 

Under the second type, there are three **Post-exorcisms**: 
The first is the method of liberation from **Incubus** and **Succubus** demons; <note>Incubi and Succubi were traditionally understood as demons that physically harassed or sexually assaulted people in their sleep.</note>
The second is the order for liberating a House disturbed by demons; 
And the third pertains to the repulsion of a storm raised by demons. 

All these things are honored, and their required efficacy is made manifest, by readings from both the Old and New Testaments, which are most aptly arranged in their proper places; so that the grace of that place may seen to be drawn from the font of the Holy Bible. And thus, we have the end of this whole work.

It would perhaps remain for other similar things to be judged in this place of the Preface for the further instruction of the Exorcist, were it not that, among many considerations, the reason of brevity also stood in the way. Therefore, let what has been related so far regarding matters pertaining to the Exorcist suffice; for I do not wish at present to deal with many opinions, perhaps silent, and...

...to respond to objections raised against this edition of mine, but I offer only this one point: namely, that no one (I pray) should disturb themselves or their reading of my work with wicked anxieties of mind. For this work of mine was not crafted for the torment of men, but for that of the Devil; but let those who rejoice <note>original: "gaudentes"</note> expect something else of a different nature.

Furthermore, I would wish to entreat all the best **Exorcists** <term>Exorcists: Clergy specifically trained and authorized to perform the rites of driving out demons.</term> that they would not disdain to put this work of mine into practice in cases of possession <note>original: "possessione." Specifically, the state of being controlled by an external spiritual entity.</note>, woven together for the glory of God and for the health of both soul and body of those **harassed by the Demon**. My hope is that in these words, with His grace assisting, they might prove the power of God and make a trial of the usefulness of this practical office.

Therefore, let them now arm themselves, and with these weapons, render themselves impregnable. Let them arm themselves, I say, for our struggle is not against flesh and blood, as **Saint Paul the Apostle** says, but against the princes and powers, and the rulers of this world of these darknesses. <note>A reference to the New Testament, Ephesians 6:12, a foundational text for the "spiritual warfare" practiced by exorcists.</note> Come now, gird yourselves, be strong in war, and fight with the **ancient serpent** <note>original: "antiquo serpente." A traditional title for the Devil or Satan, originating from the Book of Genesis and the Book of Revelation.</note>, so that you may receive the **eternal kingdom**, in which you may rejoice forever, with God the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit,

I have offended you, Lord, in thought, in delight <note>original: "delectatione." This refers to "morose delectation," or dwelling with pleasure on a sinful thought.</note>, by omission, by commission, in word, and in deed. I have acted against the ten commandments of your holy law. I have not loved Prudence, Temperance, Fortitude, and Justice <note>These are the four Cardinal Virtues, the pillars of a moral life.</note>. And finally, I have wandered astray through Sight, Hearing, Taste, Smell, and Touch; but you, who overlook the sins of men for the sake of repentance, do not turn away your face from my sobbing and my cries; do not rebuke me in your fury, nor chastise me in your anger, but have mercy on me according to your great mercy. And according to the multitude of your mercies, blot out my iniquity <note>original: "dele iniquitatem meam." This is a direct quote from the Miserere (Psalm 51/50).</note>.

I seek your great mercy because my misery is great; I have committed infinite sin, offending the infinite majesty of infinite goodness. You are good, but I have not loved your goodness with all my heart, nor with all my soul, nor with all my strength. But for this reason I ask pardon from you, because you are good; and from you alone, who are infinite, because you are infinitely able to forgive guilt. Wash me more thoroughly from my iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin. For I acknowledge my iniquity, and my sin is ever before me. # PRE-EXORCISM
## SECOND.

->The confession of the possessed person,<-
->and of those standing by.<-

*¶ The Exorcist, rising from the prayer of Confession, if he wishes to speak to those present concerning the faith, or the prayer to be held, or the nature of the Demon, let him complete this pious purpose of the soul in the name of the Lord; but if not, let him provide the book to some assistant, who, especially in the name of the possessed person and all those assisting, shall make the following general confession, while everyone is kneeling.*

> I confess to almighty God the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, God three and one, to Mary the Immaculate ever Virgin, to Saint Michael the Archangel, to the Blessed Patriarch Abraham, to David the Prophet, to Peter the Apostle, to Matthew the Evangelist, to John the Baptist, to the Innocent <note>original: "Innocenti." Refers to the Holy Innocents, the children slain by Herod, regarded as early Christian martyrs.</note>, to Stephen the First Martyr, to Silvester the Pope <note>original: "Pontifici." Referring to Pope Silvester I.</note>, to Francis the Confessor <note>term: "Confessor" - a saint who lived a life of great virtue and suffered for the faith, but was not killed as a martyr.</note>, to Ambrose the Doctor <note>term: "Doctor" - a title given to saints who significantly contributed to Church doctrine or theology.</note>, to Giles the Abbot, to Paul the first Hermit, to Crispus the Priest, to Lawrence the Levite <note>original: "Levitæ." A liturgical term for a Deacon, drawing a parallel to the Levites of the Old Testament who served in the Temple.</note>, to the Blessed Catherine the Virgin, to Monica the Widow, to all the saints, and to you, Father <note>Addressing the priest/exorcist presiding over the rite.</note>, that I have sinned gravely through my own fault against God, against myself, and against my neighbor; spiritually, carnally, mortally, venially, in heart, mouth, deed, and omission, for all of which sins I now repent. Therefore I renounce, I renounce the Devil and his works; I forgive all who have sinned against me, so that my heavenly Father may forgive me my sins; and I pray to God, to Mary ever Virgin, to all the orders of blessed spirits, the holy Patriarchs, Prophets, Apostles, Evangelists, Innocents, Martyrs, Bishops, Confessors, Doctors, Monks, Hermits, Priests, Levites, holy Virgins and Widows, and all together

<meta>catchword: with [original Latin catchword "mul" suggests the next word was "multitudine" (multitude) or "mulieribus" (women)]</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the Second Pre-Exorcism, focusing on a communal General Confession (Confiteor). It lists a wide hierarchy of saints and orders of spirits to be invoked, ensuring the possessed person and all witnesses are spiritually cleansed before the exorcism continues.</summary>

*¶ Then the Exorcist Priest, extending his hand in the manner of an imposition <note>The "imposition of hands" is a symbolic gesture of calling down the Holy Spirit or conferring a blessing.</note>, shall say over the possessed person and those standing by the following Absolution.*

<margin>Absolution of the possessed person and the bystanders.</margin>
> May Almighty eternal God, who through his son our Lord Jesus Christ said to his holy Apostles: "Whose sins you shall forgive, they are forgiven them," <note>original: "Quorum remiseritis peccata, remittuntūr eis." Amen.

<margin>Psalm 66 <note>In modern English Bibles, this is typically numbered as Psalm 67. The Latin Vulgate used different numbering for the Psalms.</note></margin>

Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, etc.

# THIRD PRE-EXORCISM,

### Which is the petition for divine help.

*¶ With a blessed small candle set upright and lit upon the candlestick, the Exorcist, while standing, shall complete the following reading, beginning it directly <note>The term "absolutè incipiendo" means starting the text without introductory liturgical greetings like "The Lord be with you."</note>.*

<margin>Esther 13 <note>The Latin "Hest. 13" refers to the Book of Esther. This prayer is part of the "additions" to Esther found in the Vulgate Bible, where Mordecai prays for God's intervention against the villain Haman.</note></margin> Lord, Lord, King almighty, for all things are placed within your power, and there is none who can resist your will, if you have decided to save Israel. You made heaven and earth, and whatever is contained within the compass of heaven; you are the Lord of all, nor is there anyone who resists your majesty. You know all things, and you know that I did not do this out of pride or contempt or any desire for glory—that I would not bow down to the most arrogant Haman. For I would have been ready to kiss even the tracks of his feet for the salvation of Israel, but I feared lest I should transfer the honor of my God to a man, and lest I should worship anyone except my God. And now, Lord King, God of Abraham, have mercy on your people, because our enemies wish to destroy us and wipe out your inheritance. Do not despise your portion, which you redeemed from Egypt. Hear my prayer, and be favorable to your lot and your measuring cord <note>original: "funiculo tuo." A biblical metaphor where a "measuring line" refers to a designated portion of land or a chosen people belonging to God.</note>, and turn our mourning into joy, so that we who live may praise your name, Lord, and do not close the mouths of those singing to you.

*¶ And immediately he shall offer the following invocation to God.*

Give, Lord God, glory to your holy name, and [hear our] prayers... <meta>catchword: prayers (mei)</meta>

Amen.

#### Which is the declaration to be made manifest to the Demons.

->*¶ With a hand placed upon the head of the possessed person, while standing, the Exorcist shall say:*<-

I, the minister and servant of God and of our Lord Jesus Christ, by the authority which I hold, exorcise you, Fathers of Lies, by the unbegotten Father; I command you, Sons of Perdition, by the Son twice begotten <note>This refers to the "dual birth" of Christ: eternally generated by the Father in heaven, and temporally born of the Virgin Mary on earth.</note> in an unspeakable manner; and I bind you, cursed spirits, by the Holy Spirit, not begotten but proceeding, by whose...

### PRE-EXORCISMS.

...of whatever rank, condition, or status you may be, you who have dared to recklessly invade this **creature of God** <note>original: "creaturam Dei." This term emphasizes that the person belongs to God by right of creation.</note>, in whatever parts of this body you are dwelling, and in whichever hiding places you are concealed; whether in essential or substantial parts, or in accidental ones <note>The text uses scholastic philosophical terms: "essential/substantial" refers to the core nature of a person, while "accidental" refers to non-essential qualities or physical symptoms.</note>; in the larger or smaller parts; the exterior or the interior; the upper or lower; the front or back; the sides, the superfluous, or the adhering parts; so that immediately, without a moment’s delay, trembling and ready, and without harm to this creature reborn through holy **baptism**, you must flee to the part to which you are called by me; and you must hear me, and hearing, perceive, and perceiving, obey. And so that no excuse remains for you on the day of judgment, before the terrifying tribunal of **Jesus Christ**, I invoke heaven and earth and everything in them as witness: if perhaps you are held by any **sorcery** <note>original: "maleficio." In this period, possession was often believed to be triggered by a "malefice" or curse performed by a third party.</note>, or incantation, knotting, detention, binding, interdiction, art, or workmanship—whether of your superiors, or of yourselves, or of evil men or women—however it was made or however it is named: I weaken, dissolve, void, annul, and destroy all those things, and I decree by the aforementioned authority that they be held as nothing.

<margin>The spirits existing within the body are commanded not to receive help, and those outside are forbidden to provide it.</margin>
Furthermore, you who have been thus released by divine authority, I command you again with an effective will that you dare not receive any help or favor, for whatever cause or under whatever pretext you wish, from the **malignant spirits** dwelling outside this body—whether you cleverly request it or they offer it to you of their own accord. To all these malignant spirits existing outside this body, of whatever order, dignity, or status they are considered, I strictly command: that they hasten to depart from here and immediately withdraw by the power of our Lord **Jesus Christ**; nor should they dare to send you any help, however small, in whatever manner or way it might be devised.

# PRE-EXORCISMS.

¶ *Here let the exorcist lift up the cross: and in a clear voice say:*

Behold the cross of the Lord; flee, you hostile powers <note>original: "partes adversæ." This refers to the demonic factions or ranks opposing God.</note>.

¶ *A second time, lifting the cross higher, he shall say in a leading voice:*

Behold the wood of the cross, upon which our salvation hung; flee, you hostile powers.

¶ *A third time, lifting the cross even higher and raising his voice, he shall say:*

Behold the wood of the Cross, upon which our salvation hung; so that he who conquered by the tree <note>A reference to the devil's victory over humanity at the Tree of Knowledge in the Garden of Eden.</note>, might also be conquered by the tree <note>A reference to Christ's victory on the wooden Cross.</note>; flee, you hostile powers.

¶ *Then he shall say the following Psalm.*

<margin>Psalm 82 <note>This is Psalm 83 in modern Bibles. It is a communal lament asking for God's protection against enemies who wish to destroy Israel.</note></margin>
O God, who shall be like you? Do not be silent, nor be restrained, O God. For behold, your enemies have made an uproar; and those who hate you have lifted up their heads. Against your people they have maliciously plotted, and they have conspired against your holy ones. They said: Come, and let us scatter them from among the nations, and let the name of Israel be remembered no more. For they have conspired with one mind; together against you they have made a covenant: the tents of the Edomites and the Ishmaelites. Moab and the Hagrites, Gebal and Ammon and Amalek; the foreigners with the inhabitants of Tyre. For even Assyria <note>original: "Assur."</note> has come with them; they have become helpers to the children of Lot. Do to them as you did to Midian, and to Sisera, and as to Jabin at the brook of Kishon. They perished at Endor, they became as dung for the earth. Make their leaders like Oreb, and Zeeb, and Zebah, and Zalmunna—all their leaders who said: “Let us possess the sanctuary of God as our inheritance.” O my God, make them like a wheel <note>original: "ut rotam." Likely referring to a rolling tumbleweed or a whirling motion like dust in a gale.</note>, and like stubble before the face of the wind. As a fire that burns the forest, and as a flame burning the mountains. So shall you pursue them in your tempest, and in your wrath you shall trouble them. Fill their faces with shame, and they shall seek your name, O Lord. Let them be ashamed and con- <note>The text breaks off mid-word "conturbentur" (be troubled), to be continued on the next page.</note>

Therefore, let it be permitted to no demon at all to despise this Exorcism of Protestation, Destruction, and Prohibition, nor to oppose it with rash daring; if anyone, however, should presume to attempt this, let him know that he shall incur immersion in the horrible blaze of fire and sulfur of the infernal hell <note>original: "gehennæ." A reference to Gehenna, the valley near Jerusalem used in the New Testament as a metaphor for the place of eternal punishment.</note>.

# THE FIFTH PRE-EXORCISM,
## Which is the summoning of the demons.

<margin>Summoning in the tongue.</margin>
¶ *So that the demons may ascend to the tongue, or (making the necessary changes <note>original: "mutatis mutandis." A Latin phrase indicating that the reader should swap specific words—like "tongue"—to fit the actual circumstances of the possession.</note>) to another part of the body; or even that they may descend, after first ringing a small bell <note>The sound of a consecrated bell was believed to disrupt demonic presence and command the spirit's attention.</note>, let the exorcist proceed:*

In the name, therefore, of the Most Holy Trinity, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit; † of the God of vengeances; strong and mighty in battle; performing judgment and justice, and rendering retribution to the proud: I, a minister of the living and true God, by the authority which I exercise, exorcize you, unclean spirits, and fortified by divine

I strictly summon you by divine exorcism so that, drawing out no delay and setting aside every other excuse, you compel that prince of darkness who holds the primary seat among you to appear—truly and personally—in the topmost part of the tongue of this creature of God, before the tribunal of our Lord Jesus Christ, the conqueror of your rashness. To him, again and again, I more strictly command by the aforementioned authority that he appear there in the manner described, without injury to the soul or to this body, so that he may give glory there to his Creator and obey me, who acts as His representative in this matter. Therefore, be obedient; and let him, ascending immediately, fear God, who did all things whatsoever He willed; who spoke, and they were made; and whom all creatures obey. as the deadlines for your appearance. In the first of these, I shall recite a Prophetic Psalm; in the second, a Prophetic Lesson; and in the third, a Gospel Lesson. From that moment on, let this body be a new hell to you, and let all my words be coals of burning fire, and the strikes of our eyes be flying arrows; and finally, may you be sent into greater torment than any other demon suffers. But now, to fulfill the first deadline, I descend with the Holy Prophet, saying:

The Prophetic Lesson appointed for this purpose.

<margin>Nahum 1</margin>
What do you imagine against the Lord? He will make an utter end. Affliction shall not rise up a second time, for as thorns embrace one another, so is the feast of those drinking together. They shall be consumed as stubble fully dry. For from you has gone forth one who plots evil against the Lord, turning over transgression in his mind. Thus says the Lord: If they were perfect and many in number, even so they shall be shorn, and he shall pass away. I have afflicted you, and I will afflict you no more. And now I will break his rod <note>original: "virgam ejus" — referring to the "yoke" or staff of the oppressor.</note> from your back, and I will burst your bonds asunder; and the Lord will give a command concerning you. No more of your name shall be sown. From the house of your God I will destroy the carved and molten image. I will make it your grave, for you are dishonored. Behold upon the mountains the feet of him that brings good tidings and announces peace. Celebrate your festivals, O Judah, and pay your vows, for Belial <note>original: "Belial" — a Hebrew term meaning "worthless" or "wicked," often used as a proper name for a high-ranking demon or the devil himself.</note> shall no more pass through you; he is utterly perished.

<margin>Psalm 138</margin>
Where do you flee? Where? Proud beast? Hear and understand, you who despise God, the prophetic voice saying: Where shall I go from your spirit, and where shall I flee from your face? If I ascend into heaven, you are there; if I descend into hell <note>original: "infernum" — in this context, it refers to the depths or the underworld.</note>, you are present. If I take my wings in the morning and dwell in the uttermost parts of the sea, even there your hand shall lead me, and your right hand shall hold me. And I said: perhaps darkness shall cover me, and the night shall be my light in my pleasures. For darkness shall not be dark to you, and night shall be as light as day; as its darkness is, so also is its light. Therefore, let dread seize you, and fear now for your stubborn rebellion, for the third sign has been established for you to appear upon the tongue, following the Holy Gospel Reading which immediately follows.

<margin>Mark 5</margin>
As Jesus went out of the boat, immediately there met him out of the tombs <note>original: "monumentis" — literally "monuments" or "memorials," referring to the rock-cut tombs where the possessed man lived.</note> a man with an unclean spirit, who had his dwelling in the tombs. And no man could now bind him, even with chains, because often he had been bound with fetters and chains, and he had burst the chains asunder and broken the fetters into pieces, and no man could tame him. And always, day and ni—

->Which concerns the use of the stole, and the oath to be<-

<margin>
What must be done if the summoned spirit does not appear.

*¶ If the summoned spirit does not appear <note>original: "ascenderit" — literally "ascend," referring to the demon manifesting or making its presence known through the possessed person.</note>, the Exorcist should rebuke and reprove him with all patience and teaching until he finally appears; but if he still neglects to appear, the exorcisms must not be stopped, once the use of the **stole** <term>stola: a long strip of fabric worn around the neck by priests, symbolizing their spiritual authority and the "yoke" of Christ.</term> has been performed as is continuously joined here. However, if he does appear, the Exorcist, standing and preparing the binding, should say the following lesson.*

### Lesson from the Book of Revelation of the blessed Apostle John.

<margin>Revelation 20</margin>
I saw an Angel descending from heaven, having the key to the abyss, and a great chain in his hand. And he seized the Dragon, the ancient serpent, who is the Devil and Satan, and bound him for a thousand years. And he cast him into the abyss and closed it, and set a seal over him, so that he should no longer seduce the nations until the thousand years were completed.

<margin>How the use of the stole is to be conducted.</margin>
¶ *When this reading is finished, the Exorcist shall place the open stole <note>A liturgical vestment consisting of a long band of colored cloth worn around the neck; here it is used as a physical symbol of spiritual authority and "binding."</note> over the left shoulder of the possessed person, hanging equally in front and behind, and tie it under the person's right arm, while saying:*

> Just as the holy Angel of God, by the power of God, bound the ancient serpent with a great chain and sealed over him; so I, by His authority, bind you, cursed creature, with this sacred stole, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit; and I mark you with the sign of the Holy Cross † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the Exorcist makes the sign of the cross.</note> so that you may cast yourself down, with all your followers, and obey God and me, His minister.

¶ *Then, standing to the left of the possessed person, preparing the oath to be given to the Demon, he shall say the following reading.*

### Reading from the Prophet Zechariah.

<margin>Zechariah 3.</margin>
The Lord showed me Joshua <note>original: "Iesum" — In the Latin Vulgate, the names "Jesus" and "Joshua" are identical. Here it refers to Joshua the High Priest from the Old Testament.</note> the High Priest standing before the Angel of the Lord, and Satan stood at his right hand to oppose him. And the Lord said to Satan: "May the Lord rebuke you, He who chose Jerusalem."

¶ *Then withdrawing and standing before the face of the possessed person, he shall say:*

### Reading of the Holy Gospel according to Luke.

<margin>Luke 9.</margin>
Having called the twelve Apostles together, Jesus gave them power and authority over all Demons, and to cure diseases. And He sent them to preach the kingdom of God and to heal the sick.

¶ *Then let the Exorcist sit, and having taken the joined hands of the possessed person between his own hands—or with the right hand of the possessed person placed upon his own hand, or upon the altar—he shall offer this Oath to the speaking Demon, inducing him to tell the truth and to offer obedience.*

<margin>When you wish to administer the Oath.</margin>
All silent stipulations of any deceitful trickery being set aside; I freely promise to you, the minister of Christ, and I confirm this promise with a binding oath.

## Which is the method for questioning the Demons.

*¶ The Sixth Pre-exorcism being completed, the Exorcist shall tell the Devil to prepare himself for two things: namely, to speak the truth and to depart from the body, while he reads through the following sacred readings: and standing, with his face turned toward the altar, he shall say:*

### Reading from the Book of Tobit.

<margin>Tobit 6.</margin>
Tobit questioned the Angel and said to him: "I beseech you, brother Azarias <note>The Archangel Raphael traveled with Tobit in the guise of a human relative named Azarias.</note>, to tell me what remedy these parts shall have, which you ordered me to keep from the fish?" And answering, the Angel said to him: "If you place a small piece of its heart upon the coals, its smoke drives out every kind of demon, whether from a man or a woman, so that it shall not approach them further. And the gallbladder is useful for anointing eyes in which there is a white film <note>original: "albugo" — a medical term for a white opacity of the cornea; the text refers to the healing of Tobit’s father’s blindness.</note>, and they shall be healed." And Tobit said to him: "Where do you wish for us to eat?" And the Angel answering said: "There is a man here named Raguel, a kinsman of your tribe, and he has a daughter named Sarah, but no male child..."

*¶ The Exorcist shall sit, and make the petitions in the following order.*

<margin>*Form for demanding something from a Demon.*</margin>
I exorcise you, wicked spirit; you who, for the sake of telling the truth voluntarily, or when questioned not hiding it, have professed by oath; that you answer me, and concerning whatever I shall ask you, you tell the truth without fraud. Answer therefore: What is your name? etc.

*¶ Here the Exorcist shall ask whatever he wishes from those things that are taught in the Preface, Chapter 10; and if he does not obtain his intent, making a continuation he should add:*

<margin>*From whence the fivefold nature <note>The text begins a theological breakdown of five ways the Grace of God operates as an authority over the demonic.</note> of grace is.*</margin>
I exorcise you so that you tell the truth in answering, by the Grace of God the highest creator, the fountain of all good, the good of every good; and who is communicatve in many forms to the creatures of His goodness.

Through the good proceeding from the effective will of the Most High, which is called His grace.

<margin>*1 Corinthians 15*</margin>
Through His general grace, by which all things exist, and consist as they are ordered toward their own natural end: Of which Saint Paul said; *By the grace of God I am what I am.* <note>original: "Gratia Dei sum id quod sum"</note>

<margin>*Ephesians 1*</margin>
Through His special grace, by which a rational creature is ordered toward their own supernatural end. Of which the Apostle said; *He predestined us to the adoption of sons to the praise of his grace.* <note>original: "Prædestinavit nos in adoptionem filiorum in laudem gratiæ suæ"</note>

<margin>*1 Corinthians 13*</margin>
Through His special grace, freely given <note>The term <term>gratia gratis data</term> refers to supernatural gifts (charisms) granted to an individual not for their own holiness, but for the spiritual benefit of others.</note>; a supernatural gift bestowed upon a rational creature, without merits, disposing them toward their own salvation, or that of another. Of which the Vessel of Election <note>original: "Vas electionis" — A traditional title for Saint Paul, referring to him as a chosen instrument of God.</note> said: *If I have prophecy, and know all mysteries* etc.

Through the grace freely given of the word of wisdom, through the divine spirit; Of the word of knowledge, according to the same spirit; Of faith, in the same spirit; Of the grace of healings, in the one spirit; Of the working of miracles; Of prophecy; Of the discerning of spirits; Of kinds of tongues, and of the interpretation of speeches. # PRE-EXORCISMS.

<margin>1 Corinthians 12</margin>
...teacher of nations) <note>original: "cator gentium" - a fragment of "praedicator gentium," a traditional title for St. Paul.</note> the operator is one and the same spirit, dividing to each one individually as he wills.

<margin>1 Corinthians 7</margin>
Through **sanctifying grace** <term>sanctifying grace (gratum facientem): a scholastic term for the supernatural gift that makes a person pleasing to God and grants them a share in divine life.</term>; a supernatural gift given to the rational creature, making them fit for salvation, and making them a temple of the Holy Spirit: Wherefore the tongue of the Holy Spirit said; *Do you not know that you are the temple of God, and the Holy Spirit dwells in you?*

<margin>1 Corinthians 10</margin>
Through **incipient grace**, which can be increased and lost: Wherefore he [Paul], aware of his own grace, said; *If anyone thinks he stands, let him see that he does not fall.*

Through **perfected grace**, which cannot be increased or lost: Wherefore that trumpet of God <note>A common honorific for St. Paul, referring to the far-reaching power of his preaching.</note> said; *The grace of God is eternal life.* <note>original: "Gratia Dei vita aeterna" from Romans 6:23.</note>

<margin>
Romans 6.
*The order of the Grace of God received in us, the diversity of its names, and its effects.*
</margin>
Through **prevenient grace**; <note>Grace that "comes before" human action to prompt a turn toward God.</note> by which we are turned away from evil: 
Through **operating grace**, by which according to the inner man we are turned toward good: 
Through **cooperating grace**, we begin to work good outwardly: 
Through **concomitant grace**, by which we persevere in the good we have begun. 
Through **subsequent grace**, by which we are drawn further along toward the perseverance of merit: either until a relapse, or until death.

Through the **necessity of God’s grace**, which a person in sin possesses so that they may turn away from evil; and after sin, so that they may persevere in good.

And finally, through the **grace** which all the saints possessed in the present world; and through the **glory** which they have in the other world, in which, enjoying God, they rejoice **for ages of ages.** <note>original: "Secula Seculorum" - the traditional liturgical conclusion meaning "forever and ever."</note>

<header>EXORCISMS.</header>

# THE PRACTICES
## OF EXORCISTS

By Brother Valerius Polidorus of Padua, of the Order of Friars Minor Conventual; Doctor of Arts and Sacred Theology; The Pre-exorcisms for the expelling of Demons and Maleficia <term>maleficia: harmful magic or "evil deeds" believed to be caused by witchcraft or demonic influence.</term> from the faithful of Christ begin.

# FIRST
## EXORCISM,

->Which is drawn from the Holy names of God.<-

*¶ The Exorcist, signing himself with the sign of the cross, shall say:*

<margin>Introduction to the exorcisms.</margin>
<note>A large decorative woodcut initial I features a central cross within an ornate frame of scrolling foliage and floral motifs.</note> MAKE me invincible, Lord, with your armor, so that I may be able to stand against the snares of the Devil. † <note>original: "armatura tua... insidias Diaboli" - This ritualizes the "Armor of God" described in the biblical Book of Ephesians 6:11.</note>

*¶ Signing himself with the sign of the cross on his forehead, he shall say:*

> Place, O Lord, upon my head the helmet of salvation to fight off diabolical attacks.

*¶ Signing himself with the sign of the cross on his chest, he shall say:*

> Clothe me in the breastplate of justice, to turn back the oppression of Satan.

*¶ Signing himself with the sign of the cross on his belly, he shall say:*

> Gird my loins with truth, to crush the authors of falsehood.

*¶ Making the sign of the cross toward his legs or his feet, he shall say:*

> Shod my feet in readiness for the Gospel of peace, to drive out those who desire war.

<sig>D 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: ¶ Making</meta>

*¶ Making the sign of the cross toward his left arm, he shall say:*

> Grant me, O Lord, the shield of firm faith, to drive back the treacherous rebels. <note>The "treacherous rebels" refers to the fallen angels or demons who rebelled against God.</note>

*¶ And slightly raising his right arm with hand closed, he shall say:*

> And give to me the sword of your Spirit, to hold victory over the tyrant. Amen. <note>The "tyrant" is a common liturgical title for Satan, emphasizing his perceived illegal or cruel rule over the possessed.</note>

*¶ Then, standing before the altar, he shall say the following Psalm.*

<margin>Psalm 93.</margin>
<note>In modern Bibles, this is Psalm 94. The numbering used here follows the Latin Vulgate tradition, which is common in older Catholic manuals.</note>
O Lord, God of vengeance; God of vengeance, act freely. Rise up, you who judge the earth; render retribution to the proud. How long, O Lord, shall the sinners—how long shall the sinners boast? They will utter and speak iniquity; all who work injustice will speak. They have humbled your people, Lord, and have vexed your inheritance. They have killed the widow and the stranger, and murdered the fatherless <term>fatherless: the original Latin "pupillos" refers to orphans or vulnerable children, signifying those most in need of divine protection.</term>. And they said, "The Lord will not see, nor will the God of Jacob understand."

Understand, you foolish among the people; and you fools, eventually learn wisdom. He who planted the ear, will he not hear? Or he who formed the eye, does he not observe? He who chastises the nations, will he not rebuke them? He who teaches man knowledge? The Lord knows the thoughts of men, that they are vain. Blessed is the man whom you have instructed, O Lord, and have taught from your law. That you may give him relief from evil days, until a pit is dug for the sinner.

For the Lord will not repel his people, and his inheritance he will not abandon. Until justice is converted into judgment, and all who are upright of heart follow it. Who will rise up for me against the wicked? Or who will stand with me against those who work iniquity? Had the Lord not helped me, my soul would have nearly dwelt in hell <note>original: "inferno" — referring here to the place of the dead or the abyss.</note>. If I said, "My foot has slipped," your mercy, O Lord, helped me. According to the multitude of my sorrows in my heart, your consolations have gladdened my soul

### LITANIES.

<margin>Many notable sayings concerning God.</margin>
Lord, have mercy. ℟. Lord, have mercy. <note>original: "Kyrie eleison" - A traditional Greek petition used in the Latin liturgy.</note>
God, God, God. ℟. Be present with us.

<margin>
The Lord’s Prayer is recited as an expanded commentary.

You who gave us the spirit of adoption in which we cry out "Abba, Father," you who are in heaven through glory, on earth through grace, and in the infernal regions through the various effects of your justice: hallowed be your name; and may it be glorified among all nations just as it has been glorified within us. May your kingdom come, and convert us; show your face and we shall be saved. Your will be done on earth as it is in heaven, and guide our steps according to your word. Give us this day our daily bread; and give us neither riches nor poverty, but grant only what is necessary for our sustenance. And forgive us our debts, just as we forgive our debtors; look upon us and have mercy on us, and forgive all our transgressions. And lead us not into the temptation of the world, in which we suffer pressure; nor of the flesh, which lusts against the spirit; nor of the Devil, who like a roaring Lion <note>original: "Leo rugiens" — A reference to 1 Peter 5:8, describing the devil's predatory nature.</note> now openly rages against us, just as at other times he lies in ambush most secretly like a savage Dragon. But you yourself are our king, who commands salvation for Jacob; through you we shall toss our enemies with a horn <note>A biblical metaphor for strength and divine power used to scatter foes.</note>, and in your name we shall despise those rising up against us; and with you as our leader, we shall trample the Lion and the Dragon. Therefore, be present at the prayers of our service, and deign to deliver this creature <note>original: "plasma" — Literally "molding" or "formation," referring to the human being as something shaped by God’s hands.</note> of yours from the enemies of your hand—this person who is harassed by your mighty power. Remember, Lord, how they once provoked you to jealousy in heaven, and how they stirred you to anger on earth, and how the pride of those who hate you rises up always. Therefore, do not permit them to harm this creature of yours as much as they wish while they possess it, nor allow them to be a nuisance to us; but deliver us from the evil of soul and body; now and forever, and for ages of ages. Amen.

<margin>Psalm 110 is declared as an introduction.</margin>
...[He is] good to the righteous through mercy; terrible to evil creatures through justice; holy to the Holy Angels in the glory of heaven; and terrible to you in the torments of hell. I, an unworthy minister of the divine majesty: I exorcise you, [N.] <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the exorcist would speak the name of the possessed person or the specific demon.</note>, you bloody beast, with all your followers: and all other evil spirits dwelling in the body of this creature of God, that you might hasten to depart from this handiwork of God—regenerated by the bath of baptism and redeemed by the precious blood of our Lord Jesus Christ—really, personally, powerfully, and effectively, with all your malice and sorceries <term>Maleficia: Acts of harmful magic or "ill-doing" believed to be the mechanical cause of the victim's suffering.</term>, and every kind of affliction; and that you go to the places prepared for you by God and suitable for you, in which you shall never again presume to harm any creature.

<margin>The Name of God, Shemhamphorasch.</margin>
I exorcise you by the incomprehensible name *Shemhamphorasch* † <note>original: "Schemhamphoras" — This refers to the "Explicit Name" of God in Jewish mysticism. Christian exorcists used it to invoke the total, hidden power of the Creator.</note>, which signifies the pure, supremely simple essence of God, with the reality of existence, the modification of infinity, and the reason of eternity; nevertheless not excluding, within the unity of the same Essence, the plurality of divine persons as it is set forth; wherefore it is truly said: Father, God; Son, God; Holy Spirit, God; The God is one and not three <note>original: "Ab, Elohim : Ben Elohim ; Ruach accades Elohim ; Haeloha echad velo scieloscia." This is a phonetic Latin rendering of Hebrew intended to show that the Trinity is hidden within the Hebrew names of God.</note>.

<margin>Eloha.</margin>
I exorcise you by the holy name *Eloha* † <note>A singular form of the Hebrew name for God, emphasizing His unique majesty.</note>, the concept of whose Deity signified by this name is of such over-reaching excellence that it cannot be fully grasped by any created intellect, but only by the infinite.

<margin>Father.</margin>
I conjure you by the name *Ab* † <note>Hebrew for "Father."</note>, from whose signified person, as if from a most fruitful Father, the Word is generated within; as the Prophet Isaiah says: "Shall I, who give generation to others, myself be sterile?" <margin>Isaiah 66.</margin> Externally, however, the universe was produced by an undivided operation with the Son and the Holy Spirit; as Malachi says: "Is there not one father of us all? Has not one God created us?" <margin>Malachi 2.</margin>

<margin>Son.</margin>
I command you by the name *Bar* † <note>Aramaic for "Son."</note>, by which the divine reality designated receives—from the one producing it, through a fruitful memory—the same numerical nature as himself, when he is generated in the day of eternity: as the Prophet says: "My Son [you are...]"

John 1.

I command you in the ineffable name *Jehovah* †, or *Tetragrammaton* <note>The "four-letter" name of God (YHWH), considered too sacred to be spoken aloud in Jewish tradition and used as a word of power in Christian exorcisms.</note> namely *Yod*, *He*, *Vav*, *He*. By the meaning of which mystery, both the trinity of divine persons and the unity of divine essence are wondrously declared: In *Yod*, which is; the Head, or Principle; understanding the Father as the principle of internal operations; and with the Son and Holy Spirit, of external ones. In *He*, which is; Being; the Son, through whom all things are; for all things were made through him and without him nothing was made that was made. In *Vav*, which is Life, or Power; the Holy Spirit, to whom power is attributed, just as Might to the Father and Wisdom to the Son. And then in *He*, which is; Being; the one divine Essence in the three aforementioned persons.

# EXORCISMS.

<margin>Jeremiah 2. Sabaoth.</margin>
I exorcise you by the name of God *Sabaoth* † <note>original: "Sabaoth" — A Hebrew title meaning "of Hosts" or "of Armies," used here to invoke God as a military commander of celestial forces against the demonic.</note> which Jeremiah, the Prophet of the Lord, made manifest while he poured out supplications to Him, saying: *But thou, O Lord of hosts, who judgest justly, and triest the reins and the hearts, let me see thy vengeance on them.* <note>original: "Tu autem domine Sabaoth, qui judicas justè..." A quote from Jeremiah 11:20 (though the margin notes chapter 2), used to call for divine justice against the possessing spirit.</note> Just as I now invoke the avenging sword against all you evil spirits, from the Lord God of armies.

<margin>Agla. Daniel 7.</margin>
I conjure you by the name *Agla* † <note>original: "Agla" — A common protective acronym in Renaissance magic and exorcism, representing the Hebrew phrase *Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai* ("Thou art mighty forever, O Lord").</note>, of omnipotent eternity and eternal power, in the mystery of the vision of the night wonderfully shown to Daniel: of one like the son of man coming in the clouds of heaven even unto the throne of the Ancient of Days who sat there, from whom he received power, and honor, and an eternal kingdom; for his power is an everlasting power that shall not be taken away, and his kingdom one that shall not be destroyed.

<margin>Adonai. Exodus 6. Amos 9.</margin>
I exorcise you, so that you may show obedience to God by departing from this his creature, through the virtue of the name *Adonai* † <note>original: "Adonai" — Hebrew for "My Lord."</note>, of which the Lord spoke to Moses, saying: *I am the Lord who appeared to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob as God Almighty, but my name Adonai I did not show to them.* <note>This refers to Exodus 6:3, where God reveals a more intimate name/identity to Moses than was known to the Patriarchs.</note> He is the one who calls the waters of the sea and pours them out over the face of the earth; The Lord is his name.

<margin>Cados. 1 Kings 2. Isaiah 6.</margin>
I conjure you by the virtue of the name *Cados* † <note>original: "Cados" — A phonetic rendering of the Hebrew *Qadosh*, meaning "Holy."</note>, of God, the Holy of Holies, Holy by unshared Holiness, Holy by his very nature: for there is none Holy as is the Lord, nor is there any other beside him, and there is none strong like our God. Wherefore the Seraphim, standing beneath the high and elevated throne of the Lord, cried out one to another: *Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord God of hosts, all the earth is full of his glory.*

<margin>Sciadai. Exodus 15.</margin>
I command you by the virtue of the name *Sciadai* † <note>original: "Sciadai" — A Latinization of *Shaddai*, usually translated as "The Almighty."</note>, of the Lord strong and powerful, the Lord mighty in battle, who worked great things in Egypt, wonders in the land of Ham, and terrible things in the Red Sea, at the departure of Israel from Egypt; while Moses sang to the Lord, saying: *The Lord is like a man of war, Almighty is his name; Pharaoh’s chariots and his army he has cast into the sea.*

I conjure you in the virtue of the name of God *Alpha and Omega* † <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, signifying that God is the beginning and the end of all existence.</note>,

# EXORCISMS.

<margin>Alpha and Omega. Revelation 22.</margin>
> ...ga †; <note>The text begins mid-word, "fonalia," likely continuing a phrase from the previous page regarding the "eternal" or "final" nature of God.</note> Alpha, I say, without Alpha, Omega without Omega: Alpha without Omega, Omega without Alpha; Alpha in itself, Omega to us; Alpha as creator, Omega as glorifier: revealed to his servant John through the Angel saying: *I am Alpha and Omega, the first and the last, the beginning and the end.* <note>original: "Ego sum Alpha & Omega..." A quote from Revelation 22:13. These first and last letters of the Greek alphabet symbolize God's totality and eternity.</note>

<margin>Agyos and Yschyros. Genesis 33.</margin>
> I command you by the power of the name of God *Agyos* and *Yschyros* † <note>original: "Agyos, & yschiros" — Latinized Greek for "Holy" and "Strong." These terms come from the ancient "Trisagion" prayer used to demonstrate God's invincible power.</note>, most celebrated and truly wonderful, according to that which was said of Jacob by Him with whom he wrestled until morning, from whom he also received a newness of name <note>A reference to Jacob being renamed "Israel" after wrestling with the angel.</note>; when Jacob inquired about His name, he received the answer from the one saying: *Why do you ask my name, which is wonderful?* And He blessed him in that same place. <note>The margin cites Genesis 33, though this event occurs in Genesis 32:29.</note>

<margin>Otheos and Athanatos. Judges 13.</margin>
> I bind you by the power of the name of God *Otheos* and *Athanatos* † <note>original: "Otheos, & Athanatos" — Latinized Greek for "God" and "Immortal."</note>, most highly commended and very miraculous, like that which Manoah asked of the Angel—who was arranging many things for the life of his wife regarding the use of food, and revealing the future—he received such a response: *Why do you ask my name, which is wonderful?* And finally, he recognized Him in the sacrifice. Therefore, by that very name, I bind you to go away from here, and to depart from this handiwork of God.

<margin>Many Hebrew and Greek names of God, etc.</margin>
> I command you, and I bind you by all these recited Holy names of God: *Schemhamphoras* † <note>original: "Schemhamphoras" — A term from Jewish mysticism (Shem HaMephorash) referring to a hidden, explicit name of God, often associated with the 72-fold name.</note>, *Eloha* <note>God</note>, *Ab* <note>Father</note>, *Bar* <note>Son</note>, *Ruach Accodes* <note>Holy Spirit</note>, *Jehovah*, *Tetragrammaton* <note>The "four-letter" name of God, YHWH</note>, *Heheje* <note>I Am</note>, *Haja Hove Vejheje* <note>He who was, who is, and who shall be</note>, *El* <note>God</note>, *Sabaoth* <note>Of Hosts</note>, *Agla* <note>A protective acronym: "Thou art mighty forever, O Lord"</note>, *Adonai* <note>Lord</note>, *Cados* <note>Holy</note>, *Sciadai* <note>Shaddai/Almighty</note>, *Alpha and Omega*, *Agyos and Yschyros*, *Otheos and Athanatos*, so that putting aside all delay, and without injury to the soul or body of this creature of God, or any other, you hasten to depart from here, leaving this person free from any of your habitation or harassment, without any deceit or fraud.

<margin>Whatever names, and in whatever way, they may be said of God.</margin>
> Finally, I exorcise you and conjure you by all other established essential names of God: whether they be essential substantives or essential adjectives; whether they be essential concrete substantives or essential abstract substantives; whether they be essential concrete adjectives or essential abstract adjectives; or personal names... <note>The exorcist is using Scholastic linguistic categories to ensure no aspect of God's identity is left out, effectively "covering all bases" so the demon cannot claim a legalistic loophole to stay.</note>

It concludes by invoking every possible linguistic category of God's name—substantive, adjective, and personal—to ensure the exorcism's total authority.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes a theological invocation based on the "notional" properties of God—technical scholastic terms for the internal relations of the Trinity—and transitions into the formal Second Exorcism. It finishes the command for demons to depart and begins a sequence of prayers and psalms meant to invoke the protection of all the saints.</meta>

# EXORCISMS.

...notional <note>original: "notionalia" — refers to "notional properties," a term in scholastic theology used to describe the internal characteristics or relationships within the Trinity (such as "begetting" or "proceeding").</note>: whether they be notional; whether they be notional-personal, or the notional nature of the notions; or finally the attributes, so that from every evil spell, of whatever condition and quality it may be; whether it was done by a Demon of its own malice, or by a man or woman through diabolical invocation; whether it was done for a time or for all eternity, you shall leave this image of God <note>The "image of God" (*imago Dei*) refers to the human person being exorcised, who is believed to carry the divine likeness.</note> set free, and do not delay to flee to the convenient places prepared for you by God.

But if you do not do this now; woe, woe, woe to you, for you leave behind the divine majesty provoked, whom it is impossible to flee, against whom no counsel is given, nor does the cleverness of deceptive simulation prevail. What then? [Name] <note>The "N." is a placeholder where the exorcist would insert the specific name of the demon being addressed.</note>, accursed prince? What do you think against the Lord? And you other evil spirits, why are you so deep of heart, that you hide your counsel from the Lord, saying: "Who sees us? And who knows us?" Perverse is that thought, as if the clay should think against the potter, and the work should say to its maker:
<margin>Isaiah 29.</margin>
"You did not make me"; and the thing formed should say to its fashioner: "You do not understand." Will He not understand, from whom no secret is hidden?
<margin>Psalm 93.</margin>
He who planted the ear, shall He not hear? Or He who formed the eye, does He not consider? And therefore He who rebukes the nations, shall He not argue against you? He shall argue, and in your rash obstinacy He shall confound you who are swallowed up, and having been confounded He shall cast you out from this body, and once cast out, He shall slaughter you in hell, for ever and ever.

## SECOND EXORCISM.
### Which is from the Order of All Saints.

->*The Exorcist, standing before the altar, shall say the following Psalm.*<-

I will sing the mercies of the Lord forever. From generation to generation: I will announce Your truth with my mouth. Since You have said: "Mercy shall be built up in heaven forever": Your truth is prepared in them. "I have made a covenant with my chosen ones, I have sworn to David my servant: even unto eternity I will prepare your seed—" <note>A recitation of Psalm 89 (Psalm 88 in the Vulgate), emphasizing God's eternal truth and the permanence of His promises against the instability of evil spirits.</note>

This specific psalm is used to establish the absolute authority of God over both the physical world and the spiritual hierarchy, serving as a legal and spiritual precedent for the exorcist's authority.</meta>

# EXORCISMS.

...forever. And I will build up your throne from generation to generation. The heavens shall confess your wonders, O Lord; and your truth in the assembly of the saints. For who in the clouds shall be equal to the Lord? Who among the sons of God <note>original: "filiis dei" — here referring to the angelic host or heavenly beings, emphasizing that even the highest spirits are subject to God.</note> shall be like unto God? God, who is glorified in the council of the saints; great and terrible above all who are round about him. O Lord God of hosts, who is like unto you? You are powerful, O Lord, and your truth is round about you. You rule over the power of the sea; and you calm the movement of its waves. You have humbled the proud one <note>original: "superbum" — in the Vulgate, this often refers to the mythical sea monster Rahab, symbolizing chaos or the enemies of God.</note> like one who is wounded; with the arm of your strength you have scattered your enemies.

Yours are the heavens, and yours is the earth; you have founded the world and the fullness thereof; the north and the sea you have created. Tabor and Hermon <note>Two prominent mountains in Israel; their mention signifies the entirety of the land and God's dominion over all directions.</note> shall rejoice in your name; your arm is with power. Let your hand be strengthened, and let your right hand be exalted; justice and judgment are the preparation of your throne. Mercy and truth shall go before your face: blessed is the people that knows jubilation. O Lord, they shall walk in the light of your countenance; and in your name they shall rejoice all the day, and in your justice they shall be exalted. For you are the glory of their strength; and in your good pleasure our horn <note>original: "cornu" — a common biblical metaphor representing strength, military might, or royal dignity.</note> shall be exalted. For our defense belongs to the Lord, and to the Holy One of Israel, our king.

Then you spoke in a vision to your saints, and said: I have laid help upon one that is mighty, and have exalted one chosen out of my people. I have found David my servant; with my holy oil I have anointed him. For my hand shall help him; and my arm shall strengthen him. The enemy shall have no advantage over him; and the son of iniquity shall not approach to hurt him. And I will cut down his enemies before his face; and them that hate him I will put to flight. And my truth and my mercy shall be with him; and in my name shall his horn be exalted. And I will set his hand in the sea; and his right hand in the rivers. He shall cry out to me: You are my father; my God, and the support of my salvation. And I will make him my firstborn, high above the kings of the earth. I will keep my mercy for him forever; and my covenant shall be faithful to him. And I will make his offspring endure for ever and ever, and his throne as the days of heaven. But if they should forsake...

<summary>This page transcribes a large portion of Psalm 88, used within the exorcism rite to proclaim God's omnipotence over creation and his unbreakable covenant with his servants. It serves to intimidate the demon by reciting the divine triumphs and the protection afforded to the faithful.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the recitation of Psalm 88 (Psalm 89 in modern Bibles) and transitions into a scriptural reading from the Book of Kings (1 Samuel). The previous page established God’s power over the natural world; this section focuses on the Davidic covenant and the subsequent suffering of the people, eventually leading to a historical precedent for demonic affliction in the story of King Saul.</meta>

# EXORCISMS.

...if his sons forsake my law; and if they have not walked in my judgments. If they profane my statutes; and do not keep my commandments. I will visit their iniquities with a rod; and their sins with stripes. But I will not take away my mercy from him; nor will I fail in my truth. Nor will I profane my covenant; and I will not make void the words that proceed from my lips. Once have I sworn by my holiness, I will not lie to David <note>original: "si David mentiar" — a traditional Hebrew idiom for making a solemn oath.</note>, his seed shall remain forever. And his throne shall be as the sun in my sight, and as the moon perfect forever; and a faithful witness in heaven. 

But you have rejected and despised; you have deferred your Anointed one <note>original: "Christum tuum" — while referring historically to the King of Israel, in this liturgical context it signifies both the suffering of the afflicted person and the promise of Christ's intervention.</note>. You have overturned the covenant of your servant; you have profaned his sanctuary on the earth. You have broken down all his hedges; you have made his stronghold a terror. All who pass by the way have plundered him; he has become a reproach to his neighbors. You have exalted the right hand of those who oppress him: you have made all his enemies rejoice. You have turned away the help of his sword; and you have not helped him in battle. You have stripped him of his purification: and you have cast his throne down to the ground. You have shortened the days of his time: you have covered him with confusion. 

How long, O Lord, will you turn away forever? Shall your anger burn like fire? Remember what my substance is: for have you created all the sons of men in vain? Who is the man who shall live and not see death? Shall he deliver his soul from the hand of the underworld <note>original: "inferi" — the realm of the dead; the prayer asks for deliverance from the power of the grave and spiritual darkness.</note>? Where are your ancient mercies, O Lord, as you swore to David in your truth? Be mindful, O Lord, of the reproach of your servants (which I have held in my bosom) from many nations. With which your enemies have reproached you, O Lord; with which they have reproached the change of your Anointed. Blessed be the Lord forever, so be it, so be it. Glory be to the Father, etc.

*¶ Turning toward the possessed person, let the Exorcist say the following reading.*

This biblical passage is foundational for exorcism as it describes the first "possessed" king of Israel.</note> from the Lord tormented him. And the servants of Saul said to him: Behold, an evil spirit from God torments you. Let our lord the King command, and your servants who are before you,

<meta>This page continues the liturgical sequence of an exorcism. While the previous page concluded with a lamentation from the Psalms regarding the Davidic line, this page transitions into the historical narrative of King Saul’s torment by an "evil spirit" and his relief through David’s music. This serves as a scriptural precedent for the use of holy words and rituals to drive away demonic influence. The text then moves into a reading from the Gospel of Matthew.</meta>

-># EXORCISMS.<-

...they shall seek a man who knows how to play the harp; so that when the evil spirit from the Lord seizes you, he may play with his hand, and you may find relief. And Saul said to his servants: "Provide me then with someone who plays well, and bring him to me." And responding, one of the youths said: "Behold, I have seen a son of Jesse the Bethlehemite who knows how to play, and he is very strong in might, a man of war, prudent in 그의 speech, a handsome man, and the Lord is with him." Saul therefore sent messengers, saying: "Send to me David your son, who is in the pastures." And so Jesse took a donkey laden with bread, a skin of wine, and one young goat from the flock, and sent them by the hand of David his son to Saul. And David came to Saul and stood before him. And Saul loved him greatly, and he became his **armor-bearer** <term>armor-bearer: Latin "armiger," a high-ranking attendant who carried the weapons of a king or commander and served as a close confidant.</term>. And Saul sent to Jesse, saying: "Let David remain in my sight, for he has found favor in my eyes." Therefore, whenever the evil spirit from the Lord seized Saul, David took the harp and struck it with his hand: and Saul was refreshed, and felt better. For the evil spirit departed from him.

*¶ With his hand placed upon the crown of the head of the possessed person, let the Exorcist say the following from the Holy Gospel.*

### Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew.

<margin>Matthew 8.</margin>
> When Jesus had come across the water into the region of the Gerasenes <note>original: "Genasenorum" — a variant of Gerasenes or Gadarenes, referring to the region near the Sea of Galilee.</note>, two men possessed by demons met him, coming out of the tombs, exceedingly fierce, so that no one could pass by that way. And behold, they cried out, saying: "What have we to do with you, Jesus, Son of God? Have you come here to torment us before the time?" Now there was a herd of many swine feeding not far from them. And the demons begged him, saying: "If you cast us out from here, send us into the herd of swine." And he said to them: "Go." And they, going out, entered into the swine. And behold, with a great rush, the whole herd went headlong into the sea, and they died in the waters. But the herdsmen fled.

<margin>Notable things said concerning God.</margin>
Be present, O true, eternal, and infinite unity of simplicity, immutability, and oneness; and in the unity of nature, be present, O three divine persons; and help us, Almighty God, whom no one precedes in eternity, nor exceeds in greatness, nor surpasses in power; for your power is an eternal power, and your kingdom is for all ages; in which you make your Angels spirits, and your ministers a burning fire; <note>Psalm 104:4. For how long shall the enemy reproach? Shall your adversary provoke you forever? Grant us this through yourself, God, our Lord and Father. Amen.

<meta>This page continues the specific ritual of exorcism. <header>EXORCISMS.</header>

# EXORCISM.

<margin>Mention of the Patriarchs. Galatians 3; Romans 4; Tobit 6.</margin>
> I exorcise you, accursed Demons, that you may take flight and fall headlong from this creature of God, who has been redeemed from your most voracious maws by the blood of Jesus Christ His son, regenerated by the water of Baptism, and numbered among the faithful in the Catholic and Apostolic Church; by the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; by the Virgin Mother of God; by all the holy choirs of blessed spirits; by the merits of all the Saints of both Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments of the Bible.</note> of whatever order: but first of the Patriarchs, and especially of Abraham the Patriarch, the father of Patriarchs, to whom the promises were made, and to his seed—he who believed, and it was reckoned to him as righteousness <note>A reference to Genesis 15:6 and Romans 4:3 regarding Abraham's faith.</note>; then by Isaac, and Jacob, and all others, in whose union the religious Tobias was admitted.

<margin>A laudable list of the Prophets. Romans 1.</margin>
> I exorcise you by the holy Prophets, who, sent by the Master of the House into the vineyard, were beaten, killed, and stoned <note>A reference to the Parable of the Tenants (Matthew 21), where the prophets are depicted as servants sent to a vineyard.</note>: Saint Hosea, Joel, Amos, Obadiah, Jonah, Micah, Nahum, Habakkuk, Zephaniah, Haggai, Zechariah, Malachi, and all others, in whom God once spoke in various ways and in many fashions. <note>original: "multifariam multisque modis," quoting the opening of the Epistle to the Hebrews.</note>

<margin>A laudable list of the Apostles. Luke 6.</margin>
> I exorcise you by the holy twelve disciples of Christ, whom He Himself named Apostles: Saint Simon, whom He surnamed Peter, and Andrew his brother; James and John; Philip and Bartholomew; Matthew and Thomas; James the son of Alphaeus and Simon, who is called the Zealot; Jude the brother of James; and Paul, teacher of the nations, who received grace and apostleship for the obedience of faith among all nations; and by all other disciples of the Lord.

<margin>The likeness of the Evangelists. Ezekiel.</margin>
> I exorcise you by the holy four Evangelists: Saint Matthew, Mark, Luke, and John, whom the Prophet Ezekiel saw in a wondrous vision in the likeness of four living creatures; the likeness of their countenances was the face of a Man, and the face of a Lion on the right of those four; and their faces and their wings were stretched out above, and the likeness of the creatures was like the appearance of torches. <note>This imagery from Ezekiel 1 is traditionally used to represent the four Gospel writers: the Man (Matthew), the Lion (Mark), the Ox (Luke), and the Eagle (John).</note>

> I exorcise you by all those who are holy and innocent of hands,

<meta>catchword: and clean</meta>

<margin>Praise of the Innocents. Revelation 14.</margin>
...and the pure in heart, who have not received their souls in vain, nor sworn deceitfully to their neighbor; and they have not been defiled with women, for they are virgins, and they follow the Lamb wherever He goes. <margin>Commendations of the Martyrs. Hebrews 11.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Martyrs, who through faith conquered kingdoms, performed justice, obtained the promises, shut the mouths of lions, extinguished the violence of fire, escaped the edge of the sword, grew strong out of weakness: they became valiant in war. Others indeed were tortured, not accepting redemption, that they might find a better resurrection; others truly experienced mockeries and scourgings, and moreover chains and prisons; they were stoned, they were sawn asunder, they were tempted, and they died by the slaughter of the sword. <note>The text invokes the "Cloud of Witnesses" from Hebrews 11, using the physical sufferings of the martyrs as a spiritual weapon against the demons.</note>

<margin>Praise of the Bishops. Titus 1.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Pontiffs <note>term: "Pontiffs" (Pontifices) refers here generally to Bishops and high-ranking prelates of the Church.</note> truly turned toward the Pastor and Bishop of their souls; and therefore without crime, as the stewards of God, they were not proud, not quick-tempered, not violent, not strikers, not greedy for filthy lucre; but as it behooves a Bishop to be, they were hospitable, kind, sober, just, holy, continent, embracing that faithful word which is according to doctrine, that he may be able to exhort in sound doctrine and to convince those who contradict; such were they.

<margin>Praise of the Confessors. Ecclesiasticus 44.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Confessors <note>term: "Confessors" (Confessores) are saints who suffered for or bore witness to the faith through their lives, but did not die as martyrs.</note>, men great in power and endowed with prudence; announcing among the Prophets the dignity of prophets, and ruling among the people of the present, and in the power of prudence giving the most holy words to the nations; who, serving the Lord in gladness, entered into His presence with exultation, and into His gates with confession. <note>original: "in confessione," which in this liturgical context means a declaration of faith and praise.</note>

<margin>Mention of the Doctors. Ecclesiasticus 4.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Doctors <note>term: "Doctors" refers to the great Teachers of the Church, such as Augustine or Jerome.</note> handed down to His holy Church by our Lord Jesus Christ; for He Himself gave some as Apostles, some as Prophets, others as Evangelists, others as Pastors and teachers <note>original: "doctores"</note>, for the perfecting of the saints. <note>Though the margin cites Ecclesiasticus, the text is actually quoting Ephesians 4:11-12.</note>

<summary>This page continues the exorcism ritual by invoking the merits and sufferings of the Holy Innocents, Martyrs, Bishops, Confessors, and Doctors of the Church to drive out demons. It emphasizes the moral purity and physical endurance of these saints as a source of spiritual authority over evil spirits.</summary>

# EXORCISMS.

...[of the saints] for the work of the ministry, for the building up of the body, until we all meet in the unity of faith. <note>This concludes the citation from the previous page (Ephesians 4:12-13), framing the exorcism as a duty performed for the health of the entire Christian community.</note>

<margin>Distinctions of the Abbots. Ecclesiasticus 44.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Abbots and Monks, men rich in virtue, having a zeal for beauty, living peacefully in their homes; for indeed all these attained glory in the generations of their nation, and are held in praise in their days.

<margin>Distinctions of the Hermits. Hebrews 11.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Hermits, who while they labored in the course of this life, wandered about in sheepskins and goatskins, being in want, distressed, afflicted—of whom the world was not worthy. Wandering in solitudes, in mountains, and dens, and in the caves of the earth: they made manifest the discipline of their teaching, and gloried in the law of the Lord's testament. <note>original: "Eremitas" – The text describes the Desert Fathers and anchorites, using the imagery from Hebrews 11:37-38 to emphasize their spiritual strength gained through physical hardship.</note>

<margin>Praise of the Priests. 2 Corinthians 6.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy Priests, men of mercy, whose piety has not failed, but in all things they have shown themselves as the ministers of God, in much patience, in tribulations, in necessities, in distresses, in stripes, in prisons, in seditions, in labors, in watchings, in fastings, in chastity, in knowledge, in long-suffering, in sweetness, in the Holy Spirit, in charity unfeigned, in the word of truth, in the power of God.

<margin>Praise of the Levites. Romans 12.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy <term>Levites</term> <note>The term "Levites" (Levitae) is used here in its traditional liturgical sense to refer to Deacons, the second rank of the major orders who assist the priest at the altar.</note>, who in their lives were haters of evil and clung to what is good; loving one another with brotherly charity, anticipating one another in honor, not sluggish in zeal, fervent in spirit, serving the Lord, rejoicing in hope, patient in tribulation, instant in prayer, sharing in the needs of the saints, and pursuing hospitality.

<margin>Praise of the Virgins. Romans 12.</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy wise Virgins, who presented their bodies as a living sacrifice, holy, pleasing to God; and proving what is the good, and acceptable, and perfect will of God; and taking up their lamps, they went out to meet the bridegroom and the bride, carrying oil with them until the coming of the bridegroom, who cries out in the middle of the night; and finally entering with him to the wedding. <note>This passage blends the exhortations of Romans 12:1 with the Parable of the Ten Virgins from Matthew 25, symbolizing the soul's preparedness for the divine.</note>

I exorcise you by all the holy Widows, who led by charity...

<margin>
The praiseworthy qualities of widows.
Romans 12.
</margin>
...of charity, in this valley of tears, they rejoiced with those who rejoiced, and wept with those who wept; being of the same mind one toward another; not minding high things, but consenting to the humble; rendering to no man evil for evil, providing good things in the sight of all men; and not overcome by evil, but overcoming evil with good. <note>This text adapts Romans 12:15-21 to describe the virtues of holy women and widows whose merits are being invoked to empower the exorcism.</note>

May all the angelic assemblies, the ranks of the Patriarchs, the merits of the Prophets, the choir of the Apostles, the blood of the Martyrs, the prayer of the Confessors, the intercessions of the Monks, the chastity of the Virgins, and the holiness of the Widows, as well as the merits of all holy men and women, be through this our humble invocation unto our help against you. May they stand before the fearsome tribunal of the living and true God as a testimony of your obstinate boldness, lest on the day of His wrath you might be able to say: "There was no one commanding us, no one conjured us." For I, the minister of God, conjure and <term>exorcise</term><note>Exorcism here functions as a legal and spiritual command to expel an occupying force (the demon) from a person (the "creature of God").</note> you by the merits of all the saints; by faith, piety, sobriety, chastity, wisdom, kindness, modesty, patience, constancy, prudence, humility, charity, examples, miracles, and perseverance. Finally and definitively, I strictly command you that you depart immediately from this creature of God, and go to the places designated for you by God, the Best and Greatest. Depart, therefore, flee you cursed ones, and withdraw, you promoters of evils. If you now neglect to do this; may fire, hail, snow, ice, and the spirit of storms be the portion of your cup, now and forever. <note>original: "spiritus procellarum" — A reference to Psalm 10:7 in the Vulgate, describing the divine punishment of the wicked.</note>

<margin>
Psalm 103.
</margin>
Bless the Lord, O my soul: O Lord my God, You are exceedingly magnified. You have put on praise and beauty, clothed with light as with a garment. Who stretches out the heaven like a skin; who covers its higher parts with waters. Who makes the cloud Your ascent, who walks upon... <note>This is Psalm 103 in the Vulgate numbering, known as Psalm 104 in most modern Bibles. It is a hymn to God's majesty in creation, used here to establish the authority of the Creator over the spiritual realm.</note>
