# EXORCISMS

...upon the wings of the winds; You who make your <note>The Latin text here reads "Angulos" (angles/corners), but in the context of Psalm 103:4, it is traditionally "Angelos" (Angels). This may be a 17th-century printing error.</note> Angels spirits, and your ministers a burning fire. You who established the earth upon its own stability; it shall not be moved forever and ever. The abyss, like a garment, is its covering; the waters shall stand upon the mountains. At your rebuke they shall flee; at the voice of your thunder they shall be afraid. The mountains rise up, and the plains descend to the place which you have established for them. You have set a boundary which they shall not pass over, nor shall they return to cover the earth.

You who send forth springs in the valleys; the waters shall pass through the midst of the mountains. All the beasts of the field shall drink; the wild donkeys shall wait in their thirst. Above them the birds of the heaven shall dwell; from the midst of the rocks they shall give forth their voices. Watering the mountains from his upper chambers; the earth shall be saturated with the fruit of your works. Bringing forth grass for the cattle, and herbs for the service of men. That you may bring bread out of the earth; and that wine may cheer the heart of man. That he may make the face cheerful with <note>The Latin text here reads "cœlo" (heaven), but the standard Psalm verse (Psalm 103:15) uses "oleo" (oil). The translator has rendered it as written while noting the likely liturgical variation.</note> heaven; and that bread may strengthen the heart of man. 

The trees of the field shall be saturated, and the cedars of Lebanon which he has planted; there the sparrows shall make their nests. The house of the heron is their leader; the high mountains are for the stags, and the rock is a refuge for the hedgehogs. He made the moon for the seasons; the sun knows its setting. You have appointed darkness, and the night is made; in it all the beasts of the forest shall go about. The young lions roaring after their prey, seeking their meat from God. The sun has risen, and they are gathered together; and they shall lie down in their dens. Man shall go forth to his work; and to his labor until the evening. 

How magnified are your works, O Lord? You have made all things in wisdom; the earth is filled with your possession. This great and wide sea; there are creeping things without number. Small animals with the great; there the ships shall pass. This dragon <note>original: "Draco". Often interpreted as Leviathan, representing the great sea monsters or chaos-beings that are nevertheless subject to God’s play and control.</note> which you formed to play in it; all expect from you that you give them food in due time. When you give it to them, they shall gather it; when you open your hand, all things shall be filled with goodness. But when you hide your face, they shall be troubled: you shall take away their spirit, and they shall fail, and shall return to their dust. Send forth your spirit, and they shall be created, and you shall renew the face of the earth. May the glory of the Lord endure forever; the Lord shall rejoice in his works...

### A Reading from the Book of Job

<margin>Job 1</margin>
On a certain day, when the sons of God <note>original: "filii Dei." In the context of Job, this traditionally refers to the angels gathered in the heavenly court.</note> had come to stand before the Lord, Satan was also present among them. To whom the Lord said: "Where do you come from?" Who, responding, said: "I have circled the earth and walked through it." And the Lord said to him: "Have you not considered my servant Job, that there is none like him on the earth, a simple and upright man, fearing God and withdrawing from evil?" 

Satan, responding to him, said: "Does Job fear God for nothing? Have you not hedged him in, and his house, and all his possessions round about? You have blessed the works of his hands, and his property has increased on the earth. But stretch out your hand a little, touch all that he possesses, [to see] if he does not curse you <note>original: "benedixerit tibi." Literally "bless you," used here as a pious euphemism in the Hebrew and Latin tradition for "curse."</note> to your face." 

Therefore the Lord said to Satan: "Behold, all that he has is in your hand; only do not stretch out your hand against his person." And Satan went out from the face of the Lord. 

Now, when on a certain day his sons and daughters were eating and drinking wine in the house of their eldest brother, a messenger came to Job, who said: "The oxen were plowing and the donkeys were grazing nearby, and the Sabeans <note>A group from the Arabian Peninsula, often depicted as raiders in the Old Testament.</note> rushed in and took everything, and they struck the servants with the sword, and I alone escaped to tell you." 

And while he was still speaking, another came and said: "The fire of God fell from heaven, and having touched the sheep and the servants, consumed them, and I alone fled to tell you." But even while he was still speaking, another came and said: "The Chaldeans <note>Ancient people from Mesopotamia, here acting as another source of disaster for Job.</note> formed three troops and invaded the camels and took them, and also struck the servants with the sword, and I alone fled to tell you." He was still speaking, and

### A Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Mark

Jesus was in the Synagogue as one having authority <note>original: "quasi potestatem habens." This passage (Mark 1:21–26) is central to exorcism because it establishes Christ's direct authority over demons, contrasted with the legalistic approach of the scribes.</note>, and not like the scribes. And there was in their Synagogue a man with an unclean spirit, and he cried out, saying: "What business is there between us and you, Jesus of Nazareth? Have you come to destroy us? I know who you are, the Holy One of God." And Jesus rebuked him, saying: "Be silent, and come out of the man!" And the unclean spirit, convulsing him <note>original: "discerpens eum," implying the spirit roughly shook or tore at the man as it was forced to depart.</note> and crying out with a loud voice, went out of him.

## EXORCISM.

<margin>
What an Angel is. See Damascus, book two, Chapter 3. <note>original: "Dam. lib. secundo. Cap. 3." Refers to St. John of Damascus’s *De Fide Orthodoxa*, which defines angels as incorporeal, intellectual beings.</note>
</margin>
I exorcise you, accursed spirits, who presume to vex this creation of God by your reckless occupation. By the same authority and with the same unwavering resolve, from the first to the last of you: let all pride be suppressed, let your arrogance and reckless audacity be cast aside. Depart truly and really from this creature of the Almighty, who was recreated by the blood of our Lord Jesus Christ. Depart together with whatever kind of witchcraft <term>Maleficio: In this context, it refers to the "spell" or "evil object" that might have facilitated the demonic entry.</term> is present, if necessary, so that immediately—whether or not a curse is involved—you may have nothing in common with this person. I command you by all the holy Angelic substances, which are created and immaterial; sharp in intellect and powerful in will; moved by love and free in choice; ministering to God and rejoicing in everlasting immortality.

<margin>
The mystery of the Trinity and Unity of God shines forth in the celestial Hierarchies.
</margin>
I exorcise you by all the holy hierarchies of blessed spirits, in which—both collectively and in each individual order—the mystery of the most Holy Trinity and the Unity of God shines forth. For just as three are together in one Holy Triumphant Church, so three divine persons exist in one most holy essence of divine substance. In the single highest order <note>The "Upper Hierarchy" in traditional angelology.</note>, the Trinity of divine persons is wonderfully contemplated in its threefold arrangement of Seraphim, Cherubim, and Thrones; just as in the single middle order, the same is perceived in its threefold arrangement of Dominations, Powers, and Principalities; and in the single lower order, in its threefold arrangement of Virtues,

<meta>This page continues the formal exorcism begun on page 84. Having invoked the general authority of the Trinity, the exorcist now systematically invokes the celestial hierarchies—Seraphim, Cherubim, Thrones, and Dominations—using the specific theological attributes of each order (Love, Knowledge, Judgment, and Authority) as spiritual weapons to command the demons to depart.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

...of him, of the Archangels and Angels, the same is declared.

<margin>How the celestial Hierarchy and its orders are illuminated in various ways.</margin>
I exorcise you again and again by the various illuminations, or the different modes of illumination, of all the celestial Hierarchies and orders. Those who are in the first Hierarchy are illuminated by God, just as those in the second are illuminated by them, and those of the third by those of the second. For the Seraphim, of the first order of the first Hierarchy, pour forth illuminations to the Dominations, of the first order of the second; and the Dominations to the Virtues, of the first order of the third. Likewise, the Cherubim, of the second order of the first, to the Powers, of the second order of the third <note>original: "Potestatibus secundi ordinis tertiæ." The text describes a complex "trickle-down" effect of divine light through the nine choirs of angels.</note>; just as the Thrones, of the third order <note>The text likely implies the third order of the first hierarchy here to complete the triad.</note>, to the Principalities, and the Principalities to the Angels, of the third order of the third.

<margin>The Seraphim exceed the other orders in love.</margin>
I adjure you by the excellence of the love of the Seraphim, by which they exceed all other lower orders with the most fervent heat of charity. They always most clearly lead those same orders toward that same love, and wonderfully bestow the form of loving while declaring the divine goodness, even though they do not turn toward anything outside of the Beloved. For they are joined immediately to God the Beloved; whence it always happens that theophanies <term>Theophany: A visible manifestation of God to humankind; here it refers to the direct reception of divine light by the highest angels.</term> are brought to them in their purest form.

<margin>The Cherubim exceed the other orders in knowledge. The order of Thrones exceeds others in the immediate understanding of the reasons for divine works.</margin>
I adjure you again by the excellence of the knowledge of the Cherubim, in whom the fullness of knowledge is not doubted, since they are joined more closely to the first Truth. Therefore, the ray of divine light shines most abundantly in them; for which reason they exceed all other angelic orders in knowledge and do not delay in advancing men toward divine understanding.

I adjure you again and again by the excellence of the immediate understanding of the reasons for the divine works of the Thrones, upon whom the Lord sits and determines the judgments of His works through them. For this reason, the righteousness of the divine judgment and the blamelessness of all works shine especially in them; receiving this through the power of God from above, they pour it forth to the lower orders.

<margin>The order of Dominations exceeds others in various ways of ruling.</margin>
I expel you by the excellence of the power of ruling of the Dominations. For they rule, first of all, by casting aside servile fear and serving the Lord—to serve whom is to reign—and then they rule over the other Angels, and finally over Men; concerning...

<meta>The catchword "domi- di" indicates the next page continues the discussion of the "dominandi" (ruling) power of the Dominations.</meta>

<summary>This page details an exorcism formula that invokes the specific powers of the first four angelic choirs—Seraphim, Cherubim, Thrones, and Dominations—to command demons. It explains the "Celestial Hierarchy," a system where divine light and authority flow from God down through ranked orders of angels to humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Celestial Hierarchy, Seraphim, Cherubim, Thrones, Dominations, illumination, divine judgment, angelic orders, theophany</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific adjurations of the angelic hierarchies. Having previously invoked the Seraphim, Cherubim, Thrones, and Dominations, the text now progresses through the Powers, Principalities, Virtues, and Archangels, citing their specific theological roles as weapons against demonic influence.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

...declaring the manner of ruling in many ways; for in spiritual warfare they teach men, admonishing prelates <note>High-ranking church officials like bishops.</note> to feed the Lord’s sheep well, and they never cease to instruct all princes in the blameless care of their subjects.

<margin>The order of Powers exceeds others by illuminating in various ways. Gregory.</margin>
> I expel you again by the excellence of the ordering of the **Powers**, through which they strive with constant care to conform the lower angels to the divine power. They direct and defend men who have been taught in the agony of spiritual struggle, so that they do not fail in the contest and lose the spiritual kingdom; meanwhile, they restrain the adverse powers <note>original: "adversas virtutes." In this context, it refers to the "hostile powers" or demons who are legally subject to the angelic order of Powers.</note> subjected to them, so that they may not cause as much harm as they desire.

<margin>Isidore: The order of Principalities exceeds others while presiding over different things in different ways.</margin>
> I expel you again and again by the excellence of the preference of the **Principalities**, according to which they arrange the subject Angels to fulfill the divine mystery. They guide the universal government of every nation and kingdom toward the likeness of God; they preside over and promote the exercise of justice among men for the glory and honor of God alone, and they most constantly teach the reverence that should be shown to each person according to the degree of their dignity.

<margin>The order of Virtues exceeds others in fortitude.</margin>
> I command you by the excellence of the fortitude of the **Virtues**, which they apply to divine operations with unshaken persistence; and having cast aside fear, they undertake all divine matters fitting to them. With tireless strength, they receive the almost infinite gifts sent to God by pious souls. By an ineffable suggestion, they take care to inform the holy pastors of the divine flock on how to rule it well; and, since God performs miracles through these spirits, they seek to conform holy men to themselves in this regard.

<margin>The order of Archangels exceeds in the announcement of the highest things.</margin>
> I command you again by the excellence of the highest announcing of the **Archangels**. For they have earned the right to explain the wonders of Christ to men; by these, indeed, the Incarnation of the Word was announced to the Virgin Mary; and when the Lord was born, "Glory in the highest and peace on earth" was wonderfully sung. The impiety of Herod against Christ was revealed by them, and His return from Egypt was advised; they ministered to Christ when He was tempted in the desert, and they comforted Him while He agonized in the garden <note>A reference to the Angel of the Agony in Gethsemane.</note>.

<meta>catchword: I command (Mando)</meta>

<meta>This page continues the systematic adjurations of the celestial hierarchy. Having moved through the higher orders (Seraphim, Cherubim, etc.) on previous pages, the text now concludes with the final order—the **Angels**—detailing their specific modes of knowledge, their unwavering commitment to goodness, and their roles as guardians of humanity.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

<sig>F 4</sig>

<margin>The order of Angels exceeds others in the demonstration of service.</margin>
> I command you again and again by the excellence of the demonstration of the service of the **Angels**: for they serve God, toward whom they strive in love, being always obedient; and they freely strengthen themselves in goodness, while also providing aid to their neighbors, since they are assigned to the protection of men, leading them to divine knowledge, and most dearly advising them to live justly.

<margin>The manifold nature of the knowledge of the Angels.</margin>
> I adjure you again by the knowledge of all the holy Angels: namely, by the **Evening knowledge** <note>original: "Vespertinam." In scholastic theology, this refers to an angel's knowledge of created things as they exist in their own nature.</note> through which they know through innate forms as they consist in their own nature; and by the **Morning knowledge** <note>original: "Matutinam." This refers to the angels' knowledge of things as they exist within the "Word" or the mind of God.</note>, by which they perceive things in the Word. But I also adjure you by the natural morning knowledge, by which they know the Word reflected through its likeness in their own nature; and the morning knowledge of glory, by which they perceive the Word through its very essence; and finally, by the knowledge which they possess through superior illumination.

<margin>The manifold ways the Angels are confirmed in goodness.</margin>
> I adjure you also by the permanence in goodness of all the holy Angels, in which they are strengthened by nature—since a spiritual creature's will is fixed after its first choice <note>The text refers to the theological belief that an angel's choice for or against God is instantaneous and permanent due to their simple, spiritual nature.</note>, for because of its simplicity, it turns itself entirely to that which it freely chooses, and has no conflicting part to pull it away; and also by Grace, since they are connected to the stable, eternal God by the never-failing bond of charity; and further by Glory, through which, since they possess God as the "Good of all good," they have no other source from which to seek goodness.

<margin>The nature of the precedence of the Angels.</margin>
> I adjure you also by the precedence <term>precedence: "prælationem," referring to the hierarchical structure of the heavenly host</term> of all the holy Angels, according to which the Angels of the first Hierarchy illuminate and perfect the Angels of the second, and those of the second, those of the third; and by these same angels, men are influenced in various ways; since some preside over regions, some over churches, and some over individual persons, until the end of the world, when their governance, which directs us to our heavenly homeland, shall cease.

<margin>The Angel speaks in various ways.</margin>
> I expel you again by the speech of all the holy Angels, which, arising from things understood in a state of wonder, represents their affections to God; and by radiating illumination, while they show the form of some deed, they speak to one another by the "word of the mind"; and 

<meta>The text breaks off here; the catchword "cum" indicates the sentence continues on the next page.</meta>

I expel you also by the guardianship of all the holy Angels over the good of man, by which they hold us up in various ways when we stand, help us when we fall, and raise us up when we have already slipped; just as they remove evils from us along with the Jebusite <note>The Jebusites were the original inhabitants of Jerusalem; in this context, the exorcist refers to the biblical tradition of angels driving out pagan or "evil" influences to prepare a holy place.</note>, they mitigate temptations with Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, they assist Elisha against visible enemies, they console Tobias in adversity, and they protect him from contrary powers, confounding those same cursed powers while driving them away.

<margin>
The number of Angels is most abundant.
Daniel 7.
Revelation 5.
</margin>
I expel you also by the most abundant multitude of all the holy Angels, filling the seats of all nine superior orders of the heavenly court with an overflowing number, while for each order he most orderly assigns a number of over six thousand, six hundred, and sixty-six legions <note>This specific number (6,666 legions) was a common medieval and Renaissance calculation for the size of the heavenly hosts, often contrasted with the "number of the beast."</note>; when Daniel received this in a vision of the night, he wrote saying: "Thousands of thousands ministered to him, and ten thousand times a hundred thousand stood before him" [Daniel 7:10]. "Thousands of thousands" was also the number of those angels whom John saw around the throne, and the living creatures, and the elders [Revelation 5:11].

<margin>
Regarding the aeviternity of angels: how they are perpetual.
</margin>
I command you again by the **aeviternity** <term>Aeviternity: In scholastic theology, the state of existence for beings like angels who have a beginning in time but no end, unlike God (who has neither) or humans (who are subject to corruption).</term> of all the holy Angels, by which—even if they had a beginning when in the beginning God created the heaven and the earth, so that they might differ from the uncreated Immaterial One who is measured by eternity—they will nevertheless never have an end. For this reason they are separated from the material creation, which is subject to decay; therefore they are not subject to continuous time, neither in regard to their essence which persists in aeviternity, nor in regard to their operations, which are measured in a discrete instant of time, by reason of which there is no indivisibility.

<margin>
The good Angels are victorious against the evil ones.
</margin>
I command you also by the victories won by all the holy Angels against you, the evil spirits, but especially by that victory in which Michael and his Angels, in reality, by will, and by the power of divine grace, through faith

->and merit<-

<meta>Catchword: Together</meta>

...and by the merit of the foreseen passion of Christ, when fighting against the most wicked Dragon, your prince, and against you—weakened as you were by your own malice—it happened that you did not prevail, but were cast down from the heights of heaven to the depths, nor did a place remain for you any longer in heaven.

<margin>The names of the Angels revealed.</margin>
I command you also by the unknown names of all the holy Angels, but especially by those revealed in Holy Scripture: And first by the name of Michael, one of the first princes who is accustomed to come to the aid of God's heavenly realm, to dispute with the Devil, and to battle the Dragon; his name is interpreted as: *Who is like God?* <note>original: "Michael: Quis ut Deus?" Michael's name is a rhetorical question in Hebrew, asserting that no one equals the Creator.</note> Then by the name of Gabriel, who declares the understanding of visions, reveals things to come concerning the Messiah and his precursor, and announces the incarnation of the divine Word; his name signifies: *The Strength of God* delivering the sentence. <note>original: "Gabriel: Fortitudinem Dei". Gabriel is traditionally the messenger of the Incarnation and the strength behind divine decrees.</note> And by the name of Raphael, one of the seven Angels standing before the Lord, the healer of blindness and the binder of the Demon; his name signifies: *The Medicine of God*. <note>original: "Raphael: Dei medicinam". Raphael appears in the Book of Tobit as a healer and a guide who binds the demon Asmodeus.</note>

And finally, I Exorcise you, who are far too proud, far too insolent, and far too reckless, by all the cursed things together: By all the Holy Angelic substances, and the Hierarchies of blessed spirits, and by their various illuminations. I adjure you by the excellence of the heat of the Seraphim <note>The Seraphim are the highest rank, defined by their "burning" love and proximity to God.</note>, the science of the Cherubim, and the immediate cognition of the reasons of divine works of the Thrones; by the knowledge of all the Holy Spirits, their perseverance in good, and their high rank. I expel you by the excellence of the power of ruling of the Dominations, the ordering of the Powers, and the precedence of the Principalities; by the speech of all the Holy spirits, the guardianship of men, and the copious multitude. Furthermore, I command you by the excellence of the strength of the Virtues, the supreme announcing of the Archangels, and the exhibition of service of the Angels; by the aeviternity <note>term: aeviternity (Latin: "aeviternitas"). A philosophical concept describing the duration of existence for created immortal beings like angels, which is mid-way between the "time" of humans and the "eternity" of God.</note> of all the holy spirits, their victories, and their names, that you depart from here immediately, and destroy all sorceries, if there be any.

Accursed one, you who obtain a contemptible primacy in this creature of God? And all you other accursed ones?

<meta>This page concludes the direct rebuke of the fallen angels regarding their loss of status and begins the Fourth Exorcism, which shifts focus to the intercession and life of the Virgin Mary, specifically utilizing the Magnificat.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

<margin>
What the devil has become because of sin.
</margin>

Are you not unrepentant together? Are you not excluded from the contemplation of God? Weakened in virtue? Blinded from the truth, though ordained by God for good? Obstinate in evil? Why do you rashly plot against God’s authority and our ministry? Let all your machinations fail; let all your sorceries be scattered, and fall away from the possession of this person, who was redeemed by our Lord Jesus Christ—the conqueror of your rashness. Just as you fell by your own fault from light into darkness, from knowledge into ignorance, from innocence into guilt, from happiness into punishment, and from the love of God and men into hatred; so too, just as heaven is no longer your seat, this creation <note>original: "plasma," referring to the human being as a thing molded or formed by God.</note> of God must not be your place. Instead, your place is in the misty regions of the dark air prepared for you by God. Depart there, withdrawing, so that after the course of the world, on the day of judgment, you may descend into eternal fire.

# FOURTH EXORCISM
### Which is drawn from the acts of the glorious life of the Virgin Mary.

*¶ While the Exorcist stands before the altar, let him say the following Psalm.*

<margin>
Luke 1.
</margin>

My soul magnifies the Lord: and my spirit has rejoiced in God my savior. Because he has regarded the humility of his handmaid: for behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed. Because he who is mighty has done great things for me: and holy is his name. And his mercy is from generation to generation to those who fear him. He has shown strength in his arm; he has scattered the proud in the conceit of their heart. He has put down the mighty from their seat, and has exalted the humble. He has filled the hungry with good things, and the rich he has sent away empty. He has taken up Israel his servant, being mindful of his mercy. As he spoke to our fathers, to Abraham, and to his seed forever. <note>This text is the "Magnificat," the song of Mary from the Gospel of Luke. In exorcism, it is used to contrast Mary's humility with the Devil's pride.</note> Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, etc.

# EXORCISMS

### Reading from the book of Genesis.

<margin>Genesis 3</margin>
The Lord God called Adam, and said to him: "Where are you?" And he said: "I heard your voice, Lord, in Paradise, and I was afraid, because I was naked, and I hid myself." The Lord said to him: "Who told you that you were naked? Have you eaten from the tree from which I commanded you not to eat?" And Adam said: "The woman whom you gave me as a companion gave to me from the tree, and I ate." And the Lord said to the woman: "Why have you done this?" She answered: "The serpent deceived me, and I ate." And the Lord said to the serpent: "Because you have done this, you are cursed among all living things and the beasts of the earth. Upon your breast shall you crawl, and you shall eat the earth all the days of your life. I will put enmities between you and the woman, and between your offspring and her offspring. She <note>original: "Ipsa." While modern translations often use "He" or "It," the Latin Vulgate used in this 1608 text uses the feminine pronoun, which the Church historically interpreted as the Virgin Mary crushing the serpent's head.</note> shall crush your head, and you shall lie in wait for her heel."

### Reading of the Holy Gospel according to Luke.

<margin>Luke 11</margin>
Jesus was casting out a Demon, and it was mute; and when he had cast out the Demon, the mute man spoke, and the crowds marveled. But some of them said: "He casts out demons by Beelzebub <note>A name derived from "Lord of the Flies," used here to refer to a prince or high-ranking chief of the demonic realm.</note>, the prince of Demons." And others, testing him, sought a sign from heaven from him. But when he saw their thoughts, he said to them: "Every kingdom divided against itself will be laid waste, and house will fall upon house. If Satan also is divided against himself, how shall his kingdom stand? Because you say that I cast out Demons by Beelzebub, by whom do your sons cast them out? Therefore they shall be your judges. But if I cast out Demons by the finger of God <note>A biblical metaphor for the power or Spirit of God.</note>, then surely the kingdom of God has come upon you. When a strong man, well-armed, guards his courtyard, all that he possesses is in peace. But if one stronger than he comes upon him and overcomes him, he will take away all his weapons in which he trusted, and distribute his spoils. He who is not with me...

<meta>This page continues the specialized Litany of the Virgin Mary from the Fourth Exorcism. The litany utilizes traditional poetic and biblical titles for Mary, but replaces the usual petition "Pray for us" with a command for the demonic entity to be confounded or thrown into confusion.</meta>

# EXORCISMS.

Mary, Vessel of honor. ℞. Confound him. <note>original: "Conf. il." (Confunde illum). In the context of an exorcism, the respondent commands the demon to be confounded or thrown into a state of confusion and shame by the power of the title invoked.</note>
Mary, house of the Lord. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, living temple. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, lily among thorns. ℞. Confound him. <note>A traditional reference to the Song of Solomon 2:2, interpreted as Mary’s purity amidst the sin of the world.</note>
Mary, rod of Jesse. ℞. Confound him. <note>A reference to the prophecy in Isaiah 11:1 regarding the lineage of the Messiah.</note>
Mary, pillar of smoke. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, sealed fountain. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, well of waters. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, unburnt bush. ℞. Confound him. <note>A reference to Moses and the burning bush (Exodus 3:2), symbolizing Mary's virginity.</note>
Mary, closed gate. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, city of God. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, enclosed garden. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, ascending through the desert. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, abounding in delights. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, fragrant with incense. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, budding olive tree. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, planting of the cedar. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, our hope. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the heavens. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Seraphim. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Cherubim. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Thrones. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Dominions. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Principalities. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Powers. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Virtues. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Archangels. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Angels. ℞. Confound him. <note>These preceding nine titles refer to the traditional Nine Choirs of Angels.</note>
Mary, queen of the Patriarchs. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Prophets. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Apostles. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Evangelists. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Innocents. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Martyrs. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Pontiffs. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Confessors. ℞. Confound him. <note>"Confessors" refers to saints who lived lives of exemplary faith and suffered for the Church but were not killed as martyrs.</note>
Mary, queen of the Doctors. ℞. Confound him. <note>"Doctors" refers to the great theologians and teachers of the Church.</note>
Mary, queen of the Monks. ℞. Confound him.

# EXORCISMS.

Mary, queen of the Hermits. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Priests. ℞. Confound him.
Mary, queen of the Levites. ℞. Confound him. <note>In this context, "Levites" refers to the secondary order of the clergy, such as deacons, drawing a parallel between the ministers of the Christian Church and the ancient tribe of Israel dedicated to God's service.</note>

Mary, queen of all Saints. Confound him.

*¶ The Exorcist, rising and standing with his face turned toward the altar, shall entreat God in the following manner:*

V. Lord, hear my prayer.
℞. And let my cry come unto thee.
V. Give us help from the Sanctuary.
℞. For the salvation of man is vain.

### PRAYER.

<margin>Notable sayings concerning God.</margin>
Eternal God, who loves as charity, knows as truth, sits as equity, dominates as majesty, reigns as prince, heals as salvation, and reveals as light: remember not the sins of the youth of this your creation, <note>original: "plasmatis tui" — literally "your formed thing." This term emphasizes the person as a vessel molded by God's hands.</note> nor his ignorances, but grant to him your mercy, which you are accustomed to give to those who ask rightly. Lord, we seek your mercy from you, because we do not wish for fire to moisten, water to burn, air to sprout, or the earth to breathe, nor for anything to perform an action contrary to its own nature, <note>The exorcist here appeals to the "Natural Law." He argues that just as it would be unnatural for fire to act like water, it is unnatural for a human being to be occupied by a demon. He asks God to restore the natural order.</note> nor to produce an effect remote from its natural cause, nor finally to ask anything from a thing that is contrary to your nature. Therefore have mercy, have mercy; and deign to snatch this your handiwork from the hands of your enemies. For does it seem good to you if you should find fault with and oppress this work of your hands, and aid the counsel of the wicked? For behold, your enemies have already made a noise, and those who hate you have lifted up their heads against her; <note>The use of the feminine "her" (eam) likely refers to the human soul (anima) or perhaps specifically to a female patient.</note> they have made a malicious plan and conspired against her. But you, Lord, throw them into disorder by the wrath of your fury, so that they may be confounded and hasten to depart from here, that they may know that your name is God. Moreover, when they have been thus confounded on account of your mercy toward us, they shall flee. For who can resist your will? We truly shall now exult and rejoice in you;

<meta>The catchword "Lauda-" indicates the next page begins with a form of the word "Laudate" (Praise), likely continuing the prayer's conclusion with joyful praise.</meta>

# EXORCISMS.

We will praise you, Lord, in your virtues, and according to the multitude of your greatness: we will praise you with the sound of the trumpet, with the choir of the heart, and the organ: we will take up a psalm, we will bring the timbrel, the pleasant psaltery, and the cithara, giving glory to your holy name, to whom there has always been, is, and will be, honor, and glory, power, and empire, throughout all ages forever. Amen.

¶ *Let the Exorcist sit here, and complete the following.*

## EXORCISM.

That undefiled Mother of God, Mary ever-virgin—whom the almighty Lord, in her being known and willed, <note>This refers to the Scholastic theological concept that God "possessed" or "knew" Mary in His divine mind before the creation of the world.</note> possessed in the beginning of his ways, before he made anything from the start; she who was ordained by him from eternity, even before the earth was made: She, and I along with her, exorcize you, O enemies of virginity, so that you may give place to her only-begotten son, and our Lord Jesus Christ. You must leave this, his dwelling-place, which you have rashly presumed to invade; and, if it be necessary, you shall depart, releasing and casting out every kind of sorcery. But can you, N. <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the exorcist would insert the specific name of the demon being addressed.</note>, accursed prince, and you other abominable spirits, resist her?

<margin>The Virgin Mary was signified in the work of the six days of creation.</margin>
She exorcizes you, she who is declared through mystery in the work of the six days:
Namely, in the **Light** created on the first day: because from her arose the Sun of Justice <note>original: "Sol justitiæ" — a common title for Christ, derived from the Book of Malachi.</note>;
In the **Firmament**, made on the second day: because she is the mediator between the angelic waters and the human waters, holding purity with the Angels, and nature with humans <note>In medieval cosmology, the "waters above the firmament" were often associated with the celestial or angelic realms, while the waters below were the earthly realm. Mary is seen here as the bridge between the two.</note>;
In the **Waters**, gathered on the third day: because in her the entire fullness of the water of grace pours itself;
In the **Moon**, along with the Sun, which receives light from it on the fourth day: because she received the splendor of innocence from Christ the Sun;
In the **Blessing** conferred upon creatures on the fifth day: because in her seed all nations are blessed;
And in the **Earth**, from which man was formed, on the sixth day: because she is the earth of which it was said: *Let the earth be opened, and bring forth a Savior.*

She exorcizes you, she who drew the beginnings of her conception without stain from the seed of Abraham, from the noble lineage of David:

# EXORCISMS.

<margin>The life, discourse of all actions, and praises of the Virgin Mary. Augustine and Cyprian.</margin>
...brought forth; <note>This continues the sentence from the previous page describing the Virgin's nature.</note> for God chose her as immaculate, and pre-selected her with every preserving grace. Therefore, whatever was done concerning her was entirely purity, entirely holiness, and entirely grace. Thus, after her own son, it is reckless to attribute any fault or sin to her; for she not only did not sin, but she could not even conceive of sin in her mind.

She <note>original: "Illa"</note> opposes you through her merits, O you most filthy dwellings of all crimes! She, who was never infected by the poisonous breaths of the ancient serpent, stood as a form of brightness and beauty. Therefore, just as sin abounded in the first woman, so in her abounded every fullness of grace. For it was fitting that, just as the earth from which the first Adam was formed was virgin and never cursed, so the earth from which the second Adam—the Lord Jesus Christ—would assume flesh should be virgin and never cursed.

<margin>Augustine.</margin>
She opposes you through her merits, whose child-bearing stood as the beginning of salvation and announced joy to the entire world. She is the flower of the field, from which the precious lily of the valleys arose. Through her childbirth, the nature of the <term>protoplasts<gloss>The "first-formed" humans, Adam and Eve.</gloss></term> is changed, and their guilt is deleted. For from her arose the Sun of Justice, Christ our God, who, by loosening the curse, gave a blessing, and by confounding death, granted us eternal life.

<margin>Augustine.</margin>
She opposes you through her merits, who, as soon as she entered the world through the lineage of the human race, shone so perfectly with the virtue of every good thing that the wisdom of God truly judged that, by coming into humanity through her, he would delete not only the sins of the first parents but of the whole world. Blessed above all women, she surpasses the Angels in purity and exceeds all Saints in piety.

<margin>The Virgin Mary was prefigured.</margin>
She commands you to flee, O sons of perdition! She who is declared figuratively in the Throne of Solomon, in the flowering rod of Aaron, in the cistern of Joseph, in the rock of the desert, in the light cloud, in the burning bush of Moses, in the bridal chamber of the bridegroom, in the exalted cypress, in the budding olive tree, in the incense smelling sweet in summer, in the ladder of Jacob, in the rising dawn, in the example of the Ark, and in many other things. <note>These references are 'types'—Old Testament symbols that medieval theologians believed specifically pointed to the Virgin Mary’s purity and role in salvation.</note>

She decrees that you flee—she of whom it was prophetically said: *The Lord shall create a new thing upon the earth, a woman shall encompass a man* <note>original: "fæmina circundabit virum," a reference to Jeremiah 31:22, though the margin notes Isaiah 7</note>. <margin>Bede</margin>
She conquers you and your stubbornness, O disciples of falsehood—she who sought the hill country and hastened to visit Elizabeth, not as if she were unbelieving of the oracle <note>Referring to the Angel Gabriel's message</note>, or doubtful of the example, but as one joyful in her vow and devout in her duty. She provides a symbolic example that every soul which conceives the Word of God in its mind immediately ascends the high peaks of virtue by the step of love.

She conquers you and your insolence—she who, after the nine months of pregnancy had passed: while a profound silence held all things, and the night was in the middle of its course; while the shepherds kept watch by night <margin>Luke 2.</margin> over their flock, she brought forth her firstborn son, and wrapped him in swaddling clothes, and laid him in a manger, because there was no room for them in the inn.

<margin>Augustine</margin>
She conquers you and your insolence, she through whose child-bearing the nature of the <term>first-formed parents<gloss>original: "protoplastorum," referring to Adam and Eve.</gloss></term> is changed and their fault is deleted. For in her was cut short that sentence of Eve’s unhappiness, in which it is said: *in pain you shall bring forth children* <note>original: "in dolore paries filios," the curse from Genesis 3:16</note>, because this woman bore the Lord in joy. For Eve mourned, but she exulted; Eve carried tears in her womb, but Mary carried joy, because the former brought forth a sinner, but the latter gave birth to an Innocent One.

She scorns you and your rashness, O conquered serpents—she who remained a virgin before the birth, during the birth, and after the birth. For just as the Word, when he was in the form of God, did not think it robbery to be equal to God, but emptied himself, taking the form of a servant, made in the likeness of men; and so, though he was God, he was made man—likewise she, though she was a virgin, was made a mother. And just as He then did not abandon his divinity, so she did not lose her virginity; and just as He is God and man, so she exists as virgin and mother.

She makes you take flight—she who, while she lived as a <term>traveler<gloss>original: "viatrix," a theological term for a person on their earthly journey toward heaven, as opposed to a "comprehensor" who has reached the destination.</gloss></term>, was capable of movement but not of sinning. For not only was the <term>tinder of sin<gloss>original: "fomes," referring to the "fomes peccati" or the innate tendency toward sin believed to be present in humans after the Fall.</gloss></term> bound, but it was extinguished in her, so that she could not stain her mind with even the slightest fault. Hence she shines above all the saints <margin>The Master [Peter Lombard], Book 3, Distinction 3.</margin> who, if they were asked while they lived here whether they were without sin, would have all answered with one voice: *If we say that we have no sin, we deceive ourselves, and the truth is not in us.*

She casts you down to the places owed to you, with me as the minister—O corrupters of the good mind—she who stood with Mary of Cleophas and Mary Magdalene near the cross of Jesus her son; when he had seen her, he said to her: *Woman, behold your son.* Then he said to his disciple John: *Behold your mother.* And from that hour the disciple took her into his own home.

<margin>The acts of the Virgin Mary's life were held in accompaniment with Christ.</margin>
She casts you down to the places owed to you, with me as the minister—she who, just as our Lord Jesus Christ her son is an advocate before the Eternal Father, who also intercedes for us with groanings that cannot be described <note>original: "gemitibus inenarrabilibus" - a biblical phrase usually applied to the Holy Spirit (Romans 8:26), here used to emphasize the intensity of Mary’s intercession.</note>, so she, as the advocate of mortals, is solicitous: for she succors the wretched, prays for the people, intervenes for the clergy, intercedes for the devoted female sex, and for the whole human race:

And she, finally, exorcises you, O enemies of virginity—she who shone forth in the acts of her life with exceeding conformity to our Lord Jesus Christ her son: for just as Christ was predestined to come in the flesh, so she was destined to give flesh to Christ: Christ was prefigured and prophesied, and she herself was prefigured and prophesied: and if the incarnation of the Word was announced, it was not to just any virgin, but to her.

She opposes you by her merits, O most filthy lodgings of all crimes—she into whom the Son of the Most High entered when he descended from the heavens: and if likewise he was conceived by nature in her without sin, so she was conceived by grace without sin <note>This reflects the 17th-century theological arguments regarding the Immaculate Conception, asserting Mary's purity from her own beginning.</note>: and just as He was born holy, so she took up a holy beginning.

<page-num>101</page-num>

# EXORCISMS.

She decrees that you must flee, O sons of perdition <note>original: "filii perditionis" — a biblical term for those destined for destruction, often applied to Judas Iscariot or the Antichrist.</note>, she who, when Christ was born, laid Him in a manger; and He was circumcised in the flesh taken from her; and when He was born, He was found by the Shepherds, where she also remained.

She tramples upon you and your pride, O companions of the unfaithful, she whose Son was found and adored in her lap by the Magi: who was carried by her into Egypt: and was found in the temple disputing among the doctors.

She overcomes you and your impudence, O disciples of falsehood, to whom the Son was subject in Nazareth: and at whose request He made wine from water in Cana: and who suffered together with the suffering Christ.

She despises you and your recklessness, O defeated Serpents, she who stood near the cross while Christ hung upon it: to whom He spoke as He talked from the cross: and whose heart was pierced by the sword of compassion <note>A reference to the prophecy of Simeon in Luke 2:35: "And a sword will pierce your own soul too."</note> when His breast was pierced with the lance.

She makes you take flight, O worshippers of sulfur, she who, just as her Son’s body did not see corruption in death, so her own dead body did not know incineration <note>original: "incinerationem" — turning to ash. This refers to the Catholic doctrine of the Assumption, stating Mary was taken body and soul into heaven.</note>: and just as Christ's body was not found in the tomb by those seeking Him, so when she passed away, she was not to be found by those who sought her: and just as Christ rose again, so she also is believed to have already risen with Him.

And she finally casts you down to the places owed to you, with me as the minister—O corrupters of the good mind—she who, just as the magnificence of Christ is elevated above the heavens, so she is exalted above the choirs of Angels to the heavenly kingdoms: and just as He intercedes for us with groanings that cannot be described <note>original: "gemitibus inenarrabilibus" — a phrase from Romans 8:26, here attributed to Mary's powerful intercession.</note>, so she prays for us: He entreats the Father, she entreats the Son: He shows His wounds, she shows her words. She, therefore, exorcises you: and I, along with her, command you by the authority of that same Son, that you hasten to depart from here: and leave this creature of God released and free from every evil spell <note>original: "maleficio" — a term for harmful magic or demonic binding.</note>.

How much longer will you hold out? Why do you delay? What are you recklessly thinking? Flee, you rebels against the eternal majesty: give place to God, you cursed ones: and depart quickly.

# FIFTH EXORCISM,
## Which is based on the deeds of our Lord Jesus Christ.

->*¶ While standing before the altar, the Exorcist shall say the following Psalm.*<-

> He who dwells in the aid of the Most High: shall abide in the protection of the God of heaven. He shall say to the Lord: You are my protector and my refuge: my God, in Him I will hope. For He shall deliver me from the snare of the hunters: and from the harsh word. With His shoulders He shall overshadow you: and under His wings you shall find hope. His truth shall encompass you with a shield: you shall not be afraid of the terror of the night, nor of the arrow that flies by day, nor of the thing that walks about in the dark, nor of the attack and the Noonday Demon <note>original: "Dæmonio meridiano" — traditionally interpreted as a spirit of spiritual sloth or "accidie," but in this context, it refers to any literal demonic presence that strikes in broad daylight.</note>. A thousand shall fall at your side, and ten thousand at your right hand: but it shall not come near you. But you shall behold with your eyes. Because you, O Lord, are my hope; you have set your refuge in the Most High. Evil shall not come to you: and the scourge shall not approach your dwelling. For He has given His Angels charge over you: to keep you in all your ways. In their hands they shall bear you up: lest you dash your foot against a stone. <margin>Luke 10</margin> You shall walk upon the asp and the basilisk <note>original: "basiliscum" — a legendary serpent king whose gaze was lethal. In medieval exorcism, these creatures represented various tiers of demonic hierarchy.</note>: and you shall trample the lion and the dragon underfoot. Because he has hoped in me, I will deliver him: I will protect him, because he has known my name. He has cried to me, and I will hear him; I am with him in tribulation: I will deliver him, and I will glorify him. I will fill him with length of days: and I will show him my salvation. Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, etc.

¶ *Turning toward the possessed person, the Exorcist shall say the following reading.*

### Reading from the Book of the Revelation of the blessed Apostle John.

There was a great battle in heaven: Michael and his Angels fought with the Dragon: and the Dragon fought, and his angels, and they did not prevail, nor was a place found for them any longer in heaven. And that great Dragon was cast down, the ancient Serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, who seduces the whole world. And he was cast down to the earth, and his angels were sent down with him. And I heard a loud voice in heaven, saying: Now salvation has come, and power, and the kingdom of our God, and the <margin>Bede <note>Reference to Saint Bede the Venerable (c. 672–735), an English monk and scholar whose commentaries on the Apocalypse were standard authoritative texts for interpreting these spiritual battles.</note></margin> authority of his Christ, because the accuser of our brothers has been cast down, who accused them before the sight of our God day and night, and they overcame him because of the blood of the Lamb and because of the word of their testimony, and they did not love their lives even unto death. Therefore, rejoice, O heavens, and you who dwell in them.

For it is written, that he has given his angels charge over you, and in their hands they shall bear you up, lest perhaps you dash your foot against a stone. ### P R A Y E R.

<margin>Notable things said concerning God.</margin>
Come now, O great, almighty, fearsome, and awesome God, who is all eye, because you see all things; all hand, because you work all things; and all foot, because you are everywhere. You who are infinite mind and a certain preeminent nature, than which nothing is better, whose providence continually governs the world which it founded: have mercy on this your creature, vexed by a demon, according to your great mercy. For are you not the Ruler, Lord God, merciful, clement, and patient? Is not your mercy greater than this person's misery? Is this not your own formation, which you milked like milk and curdled like cheese; which you clothed with skin and flesh, and joined together with bones and nerves? <note>This passage is a direct quotation from Job 10:10-11, used here to remind God of his intimate role in creating the human body now being tormented.</note> Therefore, have mercy on your image and likeness, so that the confounded Devil who occupies it, and all his satellites, may be dispersed by your power and crushed by the right hand of the strength of your arm. Being adjured by the virtue of the magnitude of your power, let them depart from here and recognize that there is no one who can resist your will, to whom all things above, in the middle, and below give glory. Hear my voice, therefore, according to your mercy, and help this your creature, so that they may be able to meditate freely on your justifications <note>original: "justificationibus" — in a liturgical context, this refers to God’s laws or righteous decrees.</note> always, and return their vows to you, the Lord, in the presence of all your people; and in the courts of your house, may they be able to praise you with unceasing thanksgiving. Amen.

### E X O R C I S M.

I exorcise you, accursed rulers of this darkness, <note>original: "rectores tenebrarum harum" — a reference to Ephesians 6:12, identifying the high-ranking demonic forces governing the fallen world.</note> by God the Word, who was in the beginning, and was with God; and who remains at the end of time: who, being what he was, took up what he was not—namely, the human nature into the divine person—when he was made flesh, in order that...

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Deuteronomy 6</margin>
<margin>Deuteronomy 6</margin>
...that laying aside all malice, insolence, and obstinacy, and hastening to depart from here for the living and true God, you may obey both Him and me, His minister, acting in His stead in this matter and so commanding. [N.], <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the exorcist would insert the name of the specific demon being addressed.</note> O damned and to-be-damned prince! It is written: *You shall not tempt the Lord your God.* <note>original: "Non tentabis Dominum Deum tuum" — a citation of Matthew 4:7, where Jesus rebukes the Devil.</note> Get back, therefore, and depart from this creation of God; <note>original: "plasmate Dei" — this term implies something molded or shaped by hand, like clay, emphasizing the intimacy of God's creation of the human body.</note> get back, and if there are any spells to be destroyed or cast out from this body, let them go. You also, other evil spirits, of whatever rank or condition you may be, why do you presume to stand against God? It is hard for you to kick against the goad. <note>original: "contra stimulum calcitrare" — a reference to Acts 9:5; it describes the useless resistance of an ox kicking against the sharp stick used to drive it.</note> For you ought to fear the One whom you despise, for it is written: *You shall fear the Lord your God, and Him only shall you serve.*

<margin>Prophecy of all the acts of the life and death of Jesus Christ.</margin>
I exorcise you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Moses, saying that the Lord said to Abraham: *In your seed all nations shall be blessed.* <note>Genesis 22:18.</note> By these words, he announced the bodily lineage of the Word-to-be-Incarnate. But also by that same most illustrious lineage of Patriarchs, Kings, and Leaders, of which the Evangelists speak in the book of His genealogy,

<margin>Amos 3</margin>
I exorcise you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Amos saying: *The Lord will not do a thing without first speaking to His prophets.* <note>Amos 3:7.</note> By these words, he said that the Word to be born in the flesh must first be announced. But also by that same Annunciation made by the Angel to the Virgin Mary, in the city of Nazareth in Galilee.

<margin>Isaiah 7</margin>
I command you to flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *Behold, a virgin shall conceive and bear a son.* <note>Isaiah 7:14.</note> By these words, he foretold the Virgin Mother of Jesus Christ. But also by that same Virgin Mother, overshadowed by the power of the Most High at her conception.

<margin>Isaiah 1</margin>
I command you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *The ox knows its owner, and the donkey its master’s manger.* <note>Isaiah 1:3.</note> By these words, he announced the manger in which Christ was to be born. But also by that same place in which Christ was laid at His birth.

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Genesis 17</margin>
I command you through the prophetic mystery revealed to the Patriarch Abraham in these words: *Every male among you shall be circumcised.* <note>original: "Omne, masculinum circumcidetur in vobis" — Genesis 17:10. This was the sign of the Old Covenant between God and Abraham's descendants.</note> By these words the circumcision of Christ was shown. But I also command you by His own circumcision, received in the flesh of His foreskin after the completion of eight days.

<margin>Psalm 71</margin>
I take away from you all power even of dwelling within this creature of God, by the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *The kings of Tarshish and the islands shall offer presents; the kings of the Arabians and of Sheba shall bring gifts.* <note>original: "Reges Tarsis, & insulae munera offerent, Reges Arabum & Saba dona adducent" — Psalm 71:10 in the Vulgate (Psalm 72:10). In Christian tradition, this is interpreted as a prophecy of the Magi visiting the infant Jesus.</note> By these words it was announced that Christ was to be adored by the Gentiles. But I also take it away by that very adoration and reverence manifested in the three Kings with gold, frankincense, and myrrh.

<margin>Malachi 3</margin>
I take away from you all power by the prophetic mystery of the words of Malachi, saying: *Presently the Lord, whom you seek, and the angel of the testament, whom you desire, shall come to his holy temple.* <note>original: "Statim veniet ad templum sanctum suum dominator, quem vos quaeritis, & angelus testamenti, quem vos vultis" — Malachi 3:1.</note> By these words he announced the presentation of Christ in the temple. But I also take it away by that same presentation, which His mother made in the temple so that she might present Him to the Lord.

<margin>Psalm 81</margin>
I take away from you all power by the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *God has stood in the assembly of gods; but in the midst He judges the gods.* <note>original: "Deus stetit in synagoga Deorum, in medio autem Deos dijudicat" — Psalm 81:1 in the Vulgate (Psalm 82:1). The text applies the "assembly of gods" to the Jewish teachers and doctors in the Temple.</note> By these words he announced the disputation Christ held in the midst of the Doctors. But I also take it away by that very disputation, in which all were astounded and marveled at His wisdom and His answers.

<margin>Ezekiel 36</margin>
I weaken you by the prophetic mystery of the words of Ezekiel, saying: *I will pour upon you clean water, and you shall be cleansed from all your filthiness, and I will give you a new spirit.* <note>original: "Effundam super vos aquam mundam & mundabimini ab omnibus inquinamentis vestris : & dabo vobis spiritum novum" — Ezekiel 36:25-26.</note> By these words he announced the baptism to be instituted by Christ. But I also weaken you by that holy baptism itself, through which, unless one is born again, he cannot enter into the kingdom of God.

<margin>Psalm 34</margin>
I weaken you by the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *I humbled my soul with fasting.* <note>original: "Operui in jejunio animam meam" — Psalm 34:13 in the Vulgate (Psalm 35:13).</note> By these words he announced the fasting of Christ. But I also weaken you by that holy...

# EXORCISMS

...the holy Fast, in the desert, consecrated for forty days and forty nights. <note>The text concludes a sentence from the previous page referring to Christ's temptation in the wilderness.</note>

<margin>Isaiah 61</margin>
I weaken you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, he has sent me to preach the gospel to sinners.* <note>original: "Spiritus domini super me, evangelizare peccatoribus misit me" — Isaiah 61:1. In the Christian tradition, this marks the start of Christ's public ministry.</note> By these words he announced that the disciples of Christ ought to be poor.

<margin>Isaiah 35</margin>
But also through those same disciples of the Lord, I will that you flee from this creature of God through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *The eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall be unstopped, then shall the lame man leap as a deer, and the tongue of the mute shall be open.* <note>original: "Aperientur oculi cæcorum, & aures surdorum patebunt, saliet sicut cervus claudus, & aperta erit lingua mutorum" — Isaiah 35:5-6.</note> By these words he announced that Christ would perform miracles. But I also command you by all those same miracles, demonstrated by Him through His powerful virtue.

<margin>Daniel 2</margin>
I will that you flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Daniel saying: *The God of heaven shall set up a kingdom which shall never be destroyed, and his kingdom shall not be delivered to another people.* <note>original: "Suscitabit Deus cæli regnum quod in æternum non dissipabitur, & regnum ejus alteri populo non tradetur" — Daniel 2:44.</note> By these words he announced the ecclesiastical state <note>This refers to the establishment of the Church as a spiritual kingdom.</note> to be instituted by Christ. But also by that same state in which there is a holy nation, a chosen race, a royal priesthood, and a purchased people.

<margin>Job 30</margin>
I will that you flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Job saying: *They have laid snares, and have prevailed against me.* <note>original: "Insidiati sunt, & prævaluerunt mihi" — Job 30:13.</note> By these words he announced the treacherous persecutions of the Jewish people to be plotted against Christ. But also by those very snares endured by Him for our sake.

<margin>Psalm 2</margin>
I nullify <term>nullify (casso): to make void or destroy the legal or spiritual force of something</term> your plans through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: *The kings of the earth stood up, and the princes met together as one against the Lord, and against his Christ.* <note>original: "Astiterunt reges terræ, & principes convenerunt in unum adversus dominum, & adversus Christum ejus" — Psalm 2:2. This is interpreted as the conspiracy of Herod, Pilate, and the high priests.</note> By these words he announced the council to be gathered against Christ. But also by that very council, in which it was determined that He Himself should die, lest the whole nation perish. <note>A reference to the words of Caiaphas in John 11:50.</note>

<margin>Psalm 54</margin>
I nullify your plans through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: *He who ate my bread has magnified his deceit against me.* <note>original: "Qui edebat panes meos magnificavit super me supplantationem" — Psalm 54:14 in the Vulgate (Psalm 55:13-14), though usually cited as Psalm 40:10 (41:9).</note> By these words he announced that Christ was to be betrayed. But also by that very betrayal enacted by Judas His disciple, which He bore for the sake of our salvation.

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Zechariah 11</margin>
I annul your plans through the prophetic mystery of the words of Zechariah saying: *They weighed out my wages at thirty pieces of silver.* <note>original: "Appenderunt mercedem meam triginta argenteis" — Zechariah 11:12. This refers to the price for which Judas Iscariot betrayed Jesus.</note> By these words he announced that our Lord Jesus Christ was to be sold. But I also annul them by that same sale, through which we have been redeemed.

<margin>Isaiah 53</margin>
I shatter you and your determinations through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *He was offered because he himself willed it.* <note>original: "Oblatus est, quia ipse voluit" — Isaiah 53:7. In the Latin Vulgate, this emphasizes the voluntary nature of Christ's sacrifice.</note> By these words he announced that Christ died of his own free will. But I also shatter you by that same spontaneous presentation of himself unto death for our sake.

<margin>Psalm 58</margin>
I shatter you through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: *They have seized my soul, the strong have rushed upon me.* <note>original: "Cœperunt animam meam, irruerunt in me fortes" — Psalm 58:4 in the Vulgate numbering (Psalm 59 in modern Bibles).</note> By these words he announced that Christ ought to be captured by the officials. But I also shatter you by that same capture, through which he released us, set free from the chains of our sins.

<margin>Micah 5</margin>
I shatter you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Micah saying: *They shall strike the jaw of the judge of Israel.* <note>original: "Percutient maxillam judicis Israel" — Micah 5:1.</note> By these words he announced that our Lord Jesus Christ was to be struck with blows, but also by that same striking—carried out by one of the officials—because he redeemed us from eternal striking. <note>The "striking" or "buffeting" refers to the physical abuse Jesus suffered during his trial before the Sanhedrin and Pilate.</note>

<margin>Isaiah 50</margin>
I decree that you flee from this creature of God through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *I have not turned my face away from those rebuking and spitting upon me.* <note>original: "Faciem meam non averti ab increpantibus & conspuentibus in me" — Isaiah 50:6.</note> By these words he announced the insult to the face of Christ after he was veiled; but I also decree it by that same insult to his face, by which he sealed upon us the light of his divine glory.

<margin>Psalm 62</margin>
I decree that you flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: *And I was scourged all day long, and my chastisement was in the morning.* <note>original: "Et fui flagellatus tota die, & castigatio mea in matutinis" — Psalm 72:14 in the Vulgate (Psalm 73 in modern Bibles). The text cites it as Psalm 62, reflecting common variations in early modern liturgical citations.</note> By these words he announced the scourging of Christ. But I also decree it by that same scourging, by which he was weakened from the sole of his foot even to the crown of his head.

<margin>Isaiah 22</margin>
I decree that you flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: *Crowning, he will crown you with tribulation.* <note>original: "Coronans coronabit te tribulatione" — Isaiah 22:18.</note> By these words he announced that Christ was to be crowned with thorns. But also by that same crown, by which he has crowned us with glory and honor.

<summary>This page contains a series of exorcism formulas that use Old Testament prophecies (from Zechariah, Isaiah, David, and Micah) to mirror the sufferings of Christ's Passion. The exorcist "shatters" and "annuls" the demon's power by citing the betrayal, arrest, and physical abuse of Jesus as the means of humanity's redemption.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the specific sequence of adjurations found in the "Thesaurus Exorcismorum." The text moves from the betrayal and capture of Christ into the specific physical sufferings of the Passion. # EXORCISMS

<margin>Psalm 94</margin>
I command you to flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *They will hunt for the soul of the righteous, and will condemn innocent blood.* <note>original: "Captabunt in animam justi, & sanguinem innocentem condemnabunt" — Psalm 94:21 (Vulgate 93:21). The exorcist uses the prophecy of Christ's wrongful trial to cast out the demon.</note> By these words he announced that Christ was to be judged unto the death of the cross. But I also command it by that same condemnation, through which we have been set free from the punishment of hell.

<margin>Micah 1</margin>
I command you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Micah, saying: *I will go stripped and naked.* <note>original: "Vadam spoliatus & nudus" — Micah 1:8. In the context of the Passion, this refers to the "Stripping of the Garments" before the crucifixion.</note> By these words he announced that Christ was to be stripped for the crucifixion. But I also command it by that same stripping, through which we have been clothed in present grace and in future glory.

<margin>Job 7</margin>
I command you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Job, saying: *My soul chooses hanging, and my bones death.* <note>original: "Eligit suspendium anima mea, & mortem ossa mea" — Job 7:15.</note> By these words he announced that Christ was to be hung upon the wood of the cross. But I also command it by that same hanging on the wood, by which he who was conquering by the wood <note>Referring to the devil, who "conquered" humanity at the Tree of Knowledge in Eden.</note> was also conquered by the wood <note>The "wood" of the Cross.</note>.

<margin>Psalm 22</margin>
I take away from you all power of staying in this body, through the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *They have pierced my hands and my feet; they have numbered all my bones.* <note>original: "Foderunt manus meas & pedes meos: dinumeraverunt omnia ossa mea" — Psalm 22:16-17 (Vulgate 21:17-18).</note> By these words he announced the wounding of the hands and feet of Christ. But I also take it away by that same wounding, through which he brought healing to the wounds of our hearts.

<margin>Isaiah 53</margin>
I take away from you all power through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah, saying: *He himself bore the sins of many, and prayed for the transgressors.* <note>original: "Ipse peccata multorum tulit, & pro transgressoribus oravit" — Isaiah 53:12.</note> By these words he announced that Christ would pray to the Father for transgressors. But I also take it away by that same prayer, through which he taught us to pray for our enemies.

<margin>Psalm 22</margin>
I take away from you all power through the prophetic mystery of the words of David, saying: *My God, look upon me; why have you forsaken me?* <note>original: "Deus, meus respice in me, quare me dereliquisti?" — Psalm 22:1 (Vulgate 21:2).</note> By these words he announced that Christ would declare the reality of his flesh upon the cross. But I also take it away through those words of his as he fulfilled the mystery, when he said: *My God, my God, why have you forsaken me?* <note>original: "Eloi, Eloi lamazabatani" — Christ's cry of dereliction in Aramaic, as recorded in the Gospels.</note>

<margin>Mark 15</margin>

### EXORCISMS.

<margin>Psalm 121</margin>
<note>While the margin cites Psalm 121, the quote is actually from Psalm 69:21 (Psalm 68 in the Latin Vulgate).</note>
[I weaken you through the mystery] of David saying: "They gave me gall for my food, and in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink": <note>original: "Dederunt in escam meam fel, & in siti mea potaverunt me aceto"</note> By these words he announced the bitter drink that was to be offered to Christ. And also by that same drink, which Christ refused after he had tasted it. <note>A reference to the Roman soldiers offering Jesus vinegar on the cross.</note>

<margin>Isaiah 53</margin>
I weaken you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: "He delivered up his soul": <note>Isaiah 53:12</note> By which he announced that Christ must destroy our death by his own death. And also by that same most precious death, through which he made us live with immortal life.

<margin>Isaiah 53</margin>
I weaken you through the prophetic mystery of Isaiah saying: "In his love and in his pity he redeemed them": <note>The text cites Isaiah 53, but this verse is Isaiah 63:9.</note> By which he announced the working of the remission of sins through Christ's death. And also by that same redemption, through which he snatched us from the jaws of hell.

I will you to flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Job saying: "He has cut me with wound upon wound; he rushed upon me like a giant": <note>Job 16:14</note> By which he announced that Christ's breast was to be wounded by a spear. And also by that same wound, from which he most openly manifested the priceless heat of his heart’s charity.

<margin>Zechariah 9</margin>
I will you to flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Zechariah saying: "As for you also, by the blood of your covenant, you have sent forth your prisoners out of the pit wherein is no water": <note>Zechariah 9:11</note> By which he announced the liberation of the Holy Fathers from Limbo to be performed by Christ. And also by that same liberation of the fathers and the despoiling of Limbo. <note>This refers to the "Harrowing of Hell," the tradition that Christ descended into the underworld to rescue the righteous souls who died before his coming.</note>

<margin>Habakkuk 3</margin>
I will you to flee through the prophetic mystery of the words of Habakkuk saying: "You have struck the head of the house of the wicked"; <note>Habakkuk 3:13</note> By which he announced the victory reported over the Devil by Christ. And also by that same victory, through which he established us as victors in glory.

<margin>Deuteronomy 21</margin>
I shatter your counsels through the prophetic mystery of the words of Moses saying: "He who is hung on a tree shall be buried that same day": <note>Deuteronomy 21:22-23. The Latin "patibulo" refers to the cross/gallows.</note> By which he prefigured the burial of Christ, whose body did not see corruption. And also by that same tomb, he who saved us from the prison of hell.

### EXORCISMS.

[of the] words of David saying: "You will not leave my soul in hell, nor will you allow your Holy One to see corruption": <note>original: "Non derelinquens animam meam in inferno, nec dabis sanctum tuum videre corruptionem" — Psalm 16:10. This is interpreted as a prophecy of Christ's resurrection.</note> By which words he announced the glorious resurrection of Christ. But also by that same resurrection, by which he established for us a firm faith in our own rising.

I frustrate your plans through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: "God has ascended with a shout, and the Lord with the sound of a trumpet": <note>original: "Ascendit Deus in jubilatione, & Dominus in voce tubæ" — Psalm 47:5.</note> By which he announced Christ's marvelous ascension into the heavens. But also by that same ascension, by which he strengthened the hope of our blessedness.

<margin>Psalm 109</margin>
<note>The marginal note refers to Psalm 109 in the Vulgate numbering, which is Psalm 110 in modern Bibles.</note>
I shatter you through the prophetic mystery of the words of David saying: "The Lord said to my Lord, sit at my right hand." <note>original: "Dixit Dominus Domino meo, sede à dextris meis."</note> By which he announced that Christ would sit at the right hand of the Father. But also by that same sitting, by which he sits at the right hand of majesty on high.

<margin>Joel 2</margin>
I shatter you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Joel saying: "I will pour out my spirit upon all flesh, and your sons and your daughters shall prophesy": <note>original: "Effundam de spiritu meo super omnem carnem, & prophetabunt filii vestri & filiæ vestræ" — Joel 2:28.</note> By which words he announced that the Holy Spirit was to be sent by Christ upon the apostles. And also by that same mission, by which the whole earth heard of his wonders and came to know divine things.

<margin>Isaiah 3</margin>
I shatter you through the prophetic mystery of the words of Isaiah saying: "The Lord will come to judgment with the elders": <note>original: "Dominus ad judicandum venies cum senioribus" — Isaiah 3:14.</note> By which he announced that Christ, the judge of the universe, would come at the end of the age. And also by his just judgment, by which he will repay each person according to their works.

And finally I **Exorcise** you, that you may obey God: I command you to flee: I take away from you all power over this creature of God, even the power of staying: I weaken you: I will you to flee: I frustrate your plans: and I shatter you, and your strengths, and all evil deeds through all the prophetic mysteries of Christ shown beforehand.

<margin>Figures of all the acts of Christ's life and death.</margin>
But also through the same Lord Jesus Christ, who was prefigured in the lineage of Isaac from Abraham; announced in Samson; <note>Samson is often viewed in Christian typology as a "type" or precursor of Christ due to his miraculous birth and his destruction of his enemies through his own death.</note> conceived in the "stone cut from the mountain without hands"; <note>A reference to the prophecy in Daniel 2:45, traditionally interpreted as the Virgin Birth.</note> placed in the manger as Moses was sent in the basket; <note>Comparing the infant Moses hidden in the reeds to the infant Jesus in the manger.</note> circumcised in Isaac; adored in Joseph; <note>Referring to the bowing of the sun, moon, and stars in Joseph's dream, or the Egyptians bowing to him, as a precursor to Christ's adoration.</note>
