...in Samuel, presented in the temple; in Solomon, found disputing in the temple; in Naaman, baptized; <note>The healing of Naaman the Syrian in the Jordan River was often seen as a prefiguration of Christian baptism.</note> in Moses, weakened by fasting; in Elisha, shown in the choosing of the disciples; in Elijah, performing miracles; in Abraham, who grew into a great nation, I am established as the Church; <note>The Latin text here shifts slightly in perspective: "ecclesiæ instituor" suggests the establishment of the Church through the lineage of Abraham.</note> in Daniel, having suffered treachery; in Jeremiah, surrounded by plots; in Amasa, betrayed; in Joseph, sold; in Judas Maccabeus, freely offered to death; in Tobit, captured; in Micaiah, struck on the cheek; in Haman, with his face covered; <note>original: "in Aman, facie velatus" — referring to Esther 7:8. Even though Haman was a villain, the detail of his face being covered before execution was used by medieval theologians as a "type" for Christ being blindfolded and mocked during the Passion.</note> in Jeremiah, scourged; in the Ram sacrificed by Abraham, crowned with thorns; in Haman, condemned to the cross; <note>In the Vulgate, Haman is hanged on a "patibulum," often translated as a cross or gallows.</note> in Jeremiah, stripped; in the Serpent, exalted on the wood; <note>Referring to the Bronze Serpent lifted up by Moses, which Christ cited as a symbol of his own crucifixion (John 3:14).</note> in King Ahab, wounded by crucifixion; <note>Ahab was pierced by an arrow (1 Kings 22:34), which is interpreted here as a type of the spear-wound in Christ’s side.</note> in Moses, an intercessor for those who troubled him; in Boaz, given vinegar to drink; <note>Refers to Boaz telling Ruth to dip her bread in vinegar (Ruth 2:14), seen as a type of the vinegar offered to Christ.</note> in Abel, slain and dead; in Moses obtaining pardon for the people, the redeemer; in Adam, wounded in the side; <note>Just as Eve was formed from Adam's side, the Church and the Sacraments were said to flow from the wound in Christ’s side.</note> in David the deliverer from the Amalekites, the deliverer of the Fathers and despoiler of Limbo; <note>original: "spoliator Limbi" — referring to the "Harrowing of Hell," where Christ was believed to have descended into the underworld after his death to free the souls of the righteous Old Testament patriarchs.</note> in Joshua, who captured the King of Jericho, the victor over the Devil; in Josiah, buried with weeping; in Samson, rising again; in Elijah, ascending into heaven; in Solomon sitting on the throne of his father, sitting at the right hand of the Father; in the Cloud filling the whole earth, the giver of the Holy Spirit; and in Daniel, he is the judge to come to judge the living and the dead and the world by fire, when he will render to each according to their works.

### Reading from the Acts of the Apostles.

<margin>Chapter 5.</margin>
By the hands of the Apostles many signs and wonders were performed among the people. And they were all with one accord in Solomon’s porch. But of the rest, no one dared to join them, but the people magnified them. And the multitude of men and women who believed in the Lord increased all the more, so much so that they even brought the sick out into the streets and laid them on beds and mats, so that as Peter came by, at least his shadow might fall on some of them, and they would all be delivered from their infirmities. A multitude also came together from the neighboring cities to Jerusalem, bringing the sick and those troubled by unclean spirits <note>original: "spiritibus immundis." In the New Testament, this refers to demons or possessing entities.</note>, and they were all healed.

### LITANIES.

Most powerful, most wise, and most excellent eternal God, under whose command the moments of our life pass, and to whom supplications are never made without hope of mercy; you who instruct us with faith—a spark of eternal radiance—and perfect us with charity—a flame of heavenly heat: by faith you illuminate, by charity you set on fire; by faith you bind, by charity you draw tight; by faith you give life, by charity you sanctify; by faith you make disciples, by charity, friends. Through faith we believe, through charity we adore; through faith we fear, through charity we love; through faith we are fellow citizens of the saints on earth, but through charity, members of the household of God in heaven. I beseech that, because of our faith and by your charity, [your] most kind ears... <note>The sentence continues on the following page.</note>

...listening [to your servant], and may your eyes, I pray, be open, so that you may hear the prayer of your servant. Be merciful to this lot, and to the cord of your inheritance <note>original: "funiculo hæreditatis tuæ." A biblical metaphor from Deuteronomy 32:9 and Psalm 16:6, referring to the people of God as His measured-out portion.</note>, which has now been made a mockery by these unclean and apostate <note>original: "apostolicis." Though the text says "apostolic," in this context of "unclean spirits," it is almost certainly a scribe's error for "apostatis" (apostate/rebel).</note> spirits. For these, having despised your power, have also scorned the commands of our ministry, treated your authority with contempt, and mocked your most reverent deeds. 

But you, our most pious, awesome, and almighty Father, turn their reproach back upon their own heads, and give them over to the contempt of all their enemies; humble them even to the earth; let burning coals fall upon them; cast them into the fire; let evils seize them in their destruction; and let their ruin be multiplied, so that they may know that your name is God, and that they may show reverence to our ministry. And so that you may make your power known to other men, along with the glory of the magnificence of your kingdom, command them to depart from here in haste; force and compel them. 

For then we—after these "riders" have been cast into the sea along with the chariots and horsemen of all their power <note>A liturgical reference to the Song of Moses in Exodus 15, here allegorizing the defeat of Pharaoh's army as the defeat of the demonic host.</note>—will sing gloriously to you, praising your name every day, and we shall bless that same Holy One forever and ever. We will speak of the magnificence of your glorious holiness and proclaim your wonders. We will tell of the power of your terrifying deeds and recount your greatness. To whom be honor and glory forever. Amen.

## EXORCISM.

I exorcise you, most wicked robbers, who despoiled the man descending from Jerusalem to Jericho, wounded him, and left him half-dead on the road <note>This opening identifies the possessing demons with the thieves in the Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30-37), where the victim represents the human soul.</note>, by the most pious Samaritan, our Lord Jesus Christ. He, suffering with an affection of mercy and drawing near, bound up his wounds, poured in oil and wine, set him on his own beast, led him to an inn, and gave two denarii to the innkeeper for him. He promised to pay back whatever more was spent; and thus, through the institution of the Holy Sacraments, he brought effective medicine, by which—the wounds of guilt itself having been healed—he might finally be led [back] to his original state.

For just as man, when he was in a state of honor, did not understand, but by sinning was compared to senseless beasts <note>A reference to Psalm 49:12: "Man, despite his riches, does not endure; he is like the beasts that perish."</note> and subjected himself through his affections to bodily things, so it was most fitting that a medicinal remedy for sin should be applied through bodily signs.

Those which existed in the Law of Nature: Offerings, Sacrifices, and Tithes, which were like a shadow; those in the Written Law: namely, Circumcision, and Expiation, and the manifold variety of other sacrifices and offerings, which were like a figure; and those which are in the Law of Grace: namely, Baptism, Confirmation, the Eucharist, Penance, Holy Orders, Matrimony, and Last Rites, which are like a most perfect fulfillment, containing within themselves the truth which they signify.

# EXORCISMS.

...just as also in the signifying of grace; in the meeting with disease; and in the healing of man.

<margin>The ancient sacraments and those of the New Law differ from each other.</margin>
I adjure you by the difference between all the Holy Sacraments of the ancient and the new law <term>New Law; the era of Christianity and the Gospel, which the author believes fulfilled and replaced the "Ancient Law" of the Old Testament</term>, which is this: those of the new law, while they are being prepared and applied, both signify and cause grace, since they possess an effective power; those of the ancient law, however, only signified it. The ancient rituals prepared the way and led toward these; but these sacraments confer what those others only showed from afar. Those were instituted to last only for a brief time, but these are to remain perpetually until the end of the world. Compared to those, these are fewer in number, greater in utility, more powerful in virtue, more worthy in preeminence, and more evident in their meaning.

<margin>What a Sacrament of the Law is.</margin>
I adjure you by the fitness of the sacraments of the new law for signifying <note>original: "significandi." In medieval theology, a sacrament is defined as a "sign" that actually does what it represents (e.g., baptismal water doesn't just represent washing, it "washes" the soul).</note>, arising from a natural similarity; through a determined order regarding a specific meaning, arising from their actual institution; and through the power of effecting that which they signify, arising from spiritual sanctification. Because of these things, a material element <term>material element; the physical substance used in a sacrament, such as water, bread, or oil</term> is truly said to be a sacrament, placed outwardly before the eyes, signifying by its institution, representing by its similarity, and conferring some invisible grace by its sanctification. Or, as it is also said: it is a visible form of invisible grace, ordained for the worship of God, bearing His image and existing as its cause.

<margin>God is the principal cause of the Sacraments.</margin>
I bind you by the principal cause of the sacraments, Almighty God; to whom the justification of the unjust (which is the interior effect of the sacraments) belongs. For the causation of the sacraments belongs to Him whose role it is to grant the sanctification of minds; and to sanctify and make sacred the minds of men belongs to the most sacred God, who is Most Holy by His unshared holiness <note>original: "imparticipata sanctitate." This implies that God's holiness is His own nature, not something He received from another.</note>, as the Apostle says: *It is God who justifies; who is he that condemns?* <note>original: "Deus qui justificat; Quis est qui condemnet?" A quote from Romans 8:33–34.</note> and as in Isaiah: *I am the Lord who sanctifies you.* <note>Though the text attributes this to Isaiah, it is a common scriptural refrain found in Exodus 31:13 and Ezekiel 20:12.</note>

<margin>The suffering of Christ is the meritorious cause of the Sacraments.</margin>
I bind you by the meritorious cause <term>meritorious cause; the action or person that earned the spiritual "merit" or "credit" that allows the sacraments to work</term> of the Sacraments, our Lord Jesus Christ, whose humanity is referred to God as a joined instrument; wherefore such is his life-giving, sacramental power...

# EXORCISMS.

...such grace must proceed into the Sacraments from Christ's divinity through His suffering humanity; for He Himself is the Angel of the Testament, who at the appointed time descended into the pool of His humanity <note>This is an allegorical reference to the Pool of Bethesda (John 5:2-4). In medieval theology, the "stirring of the water" represents Christ's Passion, which gives healing power to the "pool" of the Church's Sacraments.</note>, so that His blood and water, stirred through His passion, might acquire such power for the Sacraments, that those descending into the pool of His merit through the reception of the Sacraments would be saved from whatever infirmity of sins they were held by.

<margin>
The Sacraments have their efficacy from the passion of Christ. Romans 3.
</margin>
I constrain you by the efficacy of the Sacraments, which they have from the passion of our Lord Jesus Christ, whose power is applied to us through the Sacraments; as a significant testimony of this most certain truth, blood and water flowed from the side of our crucified Lord, wherefore God is the gracious propitiator <term>propitiator; one who reconciles or makes atonement, specifically referring here to Christ's role in satisfying divine justice</term> of all, through His grace which is in Christ Jesus, whom He set forth as a propitiator through faith in His blood, for the demonstration of His justice for the remission of preceding sins, in the forbearance of God for the demonstration of His justice in this time, so that He Himself might be just and the justifier of him who is of the faith of Jesus Christ, as Paul testifies.

<margin>
The power of excellence is in Christ.
</margin>
I command you, just as by the power of authority, insofar as He is God, so by the excellence of ministry, insofar as He is man, which our Lord Jesus Christ has in the Sacraments; since the merit of His passion works in them, and is joined to us through the faith of Christ declared in the Sacraments; since in His name the Sacraments are sanctified and conferred; since He Himself can institute them and infuse power; and without an external Sacrament, cause the effect of sanctification (as is fitting); for it is not expedient for the cause to depend on the effect, but the effect on the cause; therefore, without the Sacraments, which are the results of His own institution, He is able to cleanse whoever is infected, by the justification of His power.

<margin>
The institution of the Sacraments of the new law was fitting for Christ.
</margin>

<meta>This page continues the theological commands used during an exorcism. While the previous page focused on the Passion of Christ as the source of sacramental power, this page details the Aristotelian and Scholastic components of the Sacraments (matter, form, and purpose) to further compel the demonic forces. It draws heavily on the definitions found in St. Augustine and Peter Lombard.</meta>

...sprouting with truth, and supreme power, most abundantly flowing from the supreme power of the Father; because of His goodness He promised rewards that bring eternal happiness, because of His truth He instituted the helping Sacraments, and because of His power He restored in us the strength to fulfill His commandments.

<margin>The material cause <note>original: *Materialis causa*; In medieval philosophy, the "material cause" refers to the physical substance used in a ritual, such as water, oil, or bread.</note> of the Sacraments.</margin>
<margin>Augustine on John <note>A reference to St. Augustine’s *Tractates on the Gospel of John*, specifically the famous principle: "The word is added to the element, and there results the Sacrament."</note></margin>
I command you by the matter of all the Sacraments, by which the Sacraments are prepared—being definable in their meaning through words or signs; or through the physical element of the Sacraments, determined by divine institution. When the word is added to this element, it becomes a Sacrament, and simultaneously the "reality of the sacrament" <term>res sacramenti; the inward spiritual grace or effect produced by the sacrament, as opposed to the outward sign</term>, which is conferred in the rightly performed administration, application, and reception of the sacrament, so that we may all receive from the fullness of Jesus Christ our Lord.

<margin>The form <note>The "form" of a sacrament refers to the specific sacred words or formula spoken during the rite that gives the physical "matter" its spiritual efficacy.</note> of the Sacraments is the word.</margin>
I adjure you again by the form of all the sacraments which, drawn forth through holy words, is received by the ears and teaches us the will and promise of God. Thus, together with the elements, they possess both the clarity to signify and the power to sanctify. For while the words and elements are presented to the ears and eyes—which are the two most knowledgeable senses—they give evidence to what is being signified. Indeed, the words hold preeminence over the elements, for it is the words that sanctify the elements, so that the power of human healing may be more abundant.

<margin>The purpose of the institution of the Sacraments is manifold.</margin>
<margin>Master of the Sentences, Book 4, Distinction 1 <note>This refers to Peter Lombard (c. 1096–1160), the "Master of the Sentences." His work was the standard theological textbook of the Middle Ages. This specific section explains why God chose to use physical rituals for spiritual ends.</note></margin>
I also adjure you by the purpose of the holy institution of the Sacraments. According to the pleasure of the Creator who instituted them, this purpose was to humble man, so that he might seek salvation even through physical elements lower than himself; it was to train man through the frequent use of the sacraments, so he would not stand idle in the marketplace all day <note>A reference to the Parable of the Workers in the Vineyard in Matthew 20:6.</note>; it was to educate man through the contemplation of things felt by the senses, so that from visible things he might perceive the invisible; and it was likewise to heal man’s spirit through the application of medicine, so that what he perceived outwardly in physical form, he might believe himself to be cured of within from evil diseases, so that at last he might ascend pure to the joys of the heavenly Jerusalem.

<margin>The Sacraments are distinguished from one another.</margin>
I also adjure you by the diversity of all the sacraments, according to which some belong to the Old Law <note>Referring to the rituals of the Old Testament, such as circumcision, which were seen by medieval theologians as foreshadowing Christian rites.</note> and others to the New; some imprint a character <term>character; a spiritual and indelible mark impressed upon the soul, which means the sacrament cannot be repeated</term>, and are not repeatable—namely, Baptism, Confirmation, and Holy Orders

<meta>catchword: and Holy Orders</meta>

Baptism, Confirmation, and Holy Orders are the only three that do this.</note> and are repeatable, namely Penance <note>modern: Confession or Reconciliation</note>, the Eucharist, Marriage, and Extreme Unction <note>The "Last Rites" or Anointing of the Sick.</note>. <meta>This page continues the specific theological arguments used by the exorcist to command demons. # EXORCISMS. 125

<margin>*What the primary and common effect of the Sacraments is.*</margin>
...[according] to a certain hidden power flowing forth; which is conferred from the "work performed" <note>original: *ex opere operato*; a theological term meaning the sacrament is effective by the very fact of being administered, rather than the holiness of the priest or the recipient.</note>, on account of the divine covenant and ordination, and the power inherent in the Sacraments. This power is not contrary to "grace that makes one pleasing" <note>original: *gratum facienti gratiæ*; also known as sanctifying grace, which makes the soul pleasing to God</note>, even if it is called Sacramental grace; indeed, it is not a habit distinct from that grace, but rather it perfects it in the one possessing it. It increases grace regarding its common effects—which are to make one pleasing and to perfect the powers of the soul with virtues and gifts ordered toward various acts—and it refers those very powers to special effects through participation in the power of the Lord's Passion.

<margin>*What the specific effect of each individual Sacrament is.*</margin>
I command you again by the specific effect of each Sacrament, by which the rational soul is enlightened in various ways: for in **Baptism**, by the grace of the Holy Spirit, the soul is cleansed from Original Sin and actual crimes; in **Confirmation**, however, it is preserved in goodness by heavenly strength. But in the **Eucharist**, through spiritual refreshment, it is incorporated into the mystical body of Christ. In **Penance** <note>Confession</note>, truly, it is fully justified by the remission of actual sins committed after Baptism; but in **Holy Orders**, it is prepared for the future life by the due execution of the received office. Likewise in **Matrimony**, it is strengthened by the mutual union of minds, instructed for the receiving and raising of offspring and the avoidance of fornication; just as in **Extreme Unction** <note>Last Rites</note>, the power of the Most High is provided for the cleansing of venial sins <note>minor sins that do not fully separate a soul from God's grace</note>.

<margin>*What the Character and Ornament of the Sacraments are.*</margin>
I also command you by the twofold disposition toward Sacramental grace, one of which is the **Character** <note>an indelible spiritual mark or seal on the soul</note> in Baptism, Confirmation, and Holy Orders; and the other is the **Ornament** in the Eucharist, Penance, Matrimony, and Extreme Unction. The Character in Baptism pertains to faith brought forth; in Confirmation, to faith strengthened; but in Orders, to faith multiplied. Therefore, it is said that Baptism is for beginners, Confirmation for those progressing, and Orders for the perfect. By the first, the faithful are distinguished from the unfaithful; by the second, the strong from the weak; and by the third, the clergy from the laity. The Ornament, truly, in the Eucharist, Penance, Matrimony, and Extreme Unction of the Sacraments...

# EXORCISMS. 126

...is not [alone], nor is it the "reality of the Sacrament" <note>original: *res Sacramenti*; in scholastic theology, this refers to the ultimate spiritual benefit or inward grace of a sacrament</note> only, but together with the **Mark** <note>original: *Charactere*; a permanent spiritual seal or "character" left on the soul by sacraments like Baptism or Holy Orders</note>, it is considered the Sacrament, because it signifies "grace that makes one pleasing" <note>original: *gratiam gratum facientem*; also known as sanctifying grace, which restores the soul's relationship with God</note>, and is the "reality of the Sacrament" because it is its effect.

<margin>*Each individual Sacrament brings virtues and takes away vices.*</margin>
I command you also by the logic of any of the **Sacraments** for including good and excluding evil, according to which **Baptism** is said to lead to **Faith**, **Confirmation** to **Hope**, the **Eucharist** to **Charity**, **Penance** to **Justice**, **Holy Orders** to **Prudence**, **Marriage** to **Temperance**, and **Extreme Unction** to **Fortitude**; just as Baptism takes away original sin, Confirmation takes away weakness, the Eucharist takes away malice, Penance takes away actual sin, Holy Orders takes away ignorance, Marriage takes away concupiscence <note>original: *concupiscentiæ*; the inclination toward sin or worldly desires</note>, and Extreme Unction takes away venial sin: and thus it brings in every good of the theological and Cardinal virtues, and drives away every evil of whatever vices.

<margin>*Definition of Baptism and testimonies. Titus 3, Ezekiel 36.*</margin>
I adjure you again by the Sacrament of **Baptism**, the washing of regeneration and renewal of the Holy Spirit poured out by God upon us through Jesus Christ our Savior, so that having been justified by His grace, we might be heirs according to the hope of eternal life, of which the prophesying **Ezekiel** said: *I will pour clean water upon you, and you shall be cleansed from all your filthiness, and I will give you a new spirit;*
<margin>*John 3.*</margin>
Therefore, Christ the Teacher affirmed concerning the same, saying: *Unless a man be born again of water and the Holy Spirit, he cannot enter into the kingdom of God:*
<margin>*Ephesians 5.*</margin>

<margin>Definition and testimonies of Marriage.</margin>
I bind you again and again by the **Sacrament of Marriage**, the lawful consent of male and female to maintain an inseparable partnership of life <note>original: *individuum vitæ consuetudinem*; this is a classic legal definition of marriage in the Western tradition, emphasizing that the union cannot be divided.</note> between themselves; of which the Lord said: "He who made man from the beginning, made them male and female," and said: "For this reason a man shall leave father and mother and shall cleave to his wife, and they shall be two in one flesh." Therefore they are no longer two, but one flesh. What therefore God has joined, let no man separate.

<margin>1 Corinthians 7.</margin>
Paul also makes mention of this, saying: "To those who are joined in marriage, I command—not I, but the Lord—that the wife not depart from her husband; but if she should depart, let her remain unmarried, or be reconciled to her husband."

<margin>Definition and testimonies of Extreme Unction.</margin>

Let him bring in the priests of the Church, that they may pray over him, anointing him with holy oil in the name of the Lord; and the prayer of faith will save the sick person, and the Lord will alleviate him, and if he be in sins, they shall be forgiven him." This 

<margin>Mark 6.</margin>
of which James speaks, the other Apostles also used; for going out they preached that people should do penance, and they cast out many demons, and they anointed many sick people with oil, and they were healed.

...essence <note>original: *quiditatem*; a philosophical term meaning "what-ness" or the essential nature of a thing.</note>: by their principal and meritorious cause; by their efficacy and the power of excellence required for their very being; by their beginning from Christ and their material cause; by their formal and final cause; by their diversity and symbolism, and by the actual participation in each of them and the principal effect common to all: What then? # THE SEVENTH EXORCISM.
### Which is drawn from the principal Dogmas of the Holy Church.

->*¶ The Exorcist should stand before the Altar and recite the following Psalm.*<-

<margin>Psalm 5.</margin>
Give ear to my words, O Lord; understand my cry. Attend to the voice of my prayer, my King and my God. For to you I will pray, O Lord; in the morning you will hear my voice. In the morning I will stand before you and see, for you are not a God who wills iniquity. Neither shall the wicked dwell near you, nor shall the unjust remain before your eyes. You have hated all who work iniquity; you will destroy all who speak a lie. The Lord will abhor the man of blood and deceit; but I, in the multitude of your mercy, will enter into your house; I will worship at your holy temple in your fear. Lead me, O Lord, in your justice because of my enemies; direct my way in your sight. For there is no truth in their mouth; their heart is vain. Their throat is an open sepulcher; they dealt deceitfully with their tongues. Judge them, O God. Let them fall from their thoughts; according to the multitude of their impieties cast them out, for they have provoked you, O Lord. And let all who hope in you rejoice; they shall exult forever and you shall dwell in them. And all who love your name shall glory in you, for you will bless the just man. O Lord, you have crowned us with the shield of your good will. Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, etc. <note>The use of Psalm 5 is strategic; it appeals to God as a protector against "the wicked" and "the deceitful," which the exorcist identifies as the demonic presence.</note>

->*¶ Having turned his face toward the possessed person <note>original: *persona obsessā*; literally "obsessed person," though in this period "obsession" and "possession" were often used interchangeably for those under demonic influence.</note>, the Exorcist shall read the following Lesson.*<-

### Lesson from the book of Job.

"...traversed the earth and walked through it." And the Lord said to Satan: "Have you considered my servant Job, that there is none like him on the earth? A simple and upright man, fearing God and turning away from evil, and still holding onto his innocence? But you have moved me against him, to afflict him without cause." To which Satan, answering, said: "Skin for skin, and all that a man has, he will give for his soul. <note>original: "Pellem pro pelle." He said to her: "You have spoken like one of the foolish women. If we have received good things from the hand of the Lord, why should we not endure evil?" In all these things, Job did not sin with his lips.

### A Reading from the Holy Gospel According to Matthew.

<margin>Chapter 17.</margin>
When Jesus had come to the crowd, a man approached him, falling on his knees before him, saying: "Lord, have mercy on my son, for he is a <term>lunatic<note>original: "Lunaticus." In the 17th century, "lunacy" was a diagnosis that blurred the lines between epilepsy and demonic oppression, often believed to be influenced by the phases of the moon.</note></term> and suffers badly. For he often falls into the fire, and frequently into the water, and I offered him to your disciples, and they could not cure him." Jesus, answering, said: "O faithless and perverse generation, how long shall I be with you? How long shall I suffer you? Bring him here to me." And Jesus rebuked him, and the <term>demon<note>original: "Dæmonium."</note></term> went out from him, and the boy was cured from that hour.

### P R A Y E R.

<margin>*A comparison of love and fear.*</margin>
O God, most worthy of love and greatly to be feared, almighty and eternal, who, by the abundance of Your piety, sweetly compels Your human creation to strive toward its ultimate end by disposing it through the stings of love and fear: for You terrify with fear, but allure with love; You embitter with fear, but soothe with love; by fear You reveal the image of punishment, but by love You propose the goal of reward; by fear You loosen us from crime, and by love You lead us to justice; by fear You bring back the strayed, and by love You lead in the returned; by fe—

<meta>This page concludes the prayer regarding the dual nature of divine fear and love and transitions into the formal sitting exorcism. The catchword "inseditis" (you have settled/occupied) from the bottom of the page links to the start of the next page's command.</meta>

<header>EXORCISMS.</header>

By fear you prevent withdrawal, and by love you confirm access; through fear you make one provident, and through love you return one to a state of caution; you humble through fear, and you gladden through love. We humbly implore the clemency of Your majesty, that You might deign to gladden with Your love this Your creature <note>original: "facturam tuam." In this context, it refers to the person being exorcised, seen as a "work" or "creation" of God.</note> who has long been shaken by fear, and humbled by excessive trembling under demonic occupation. May the effects of Your love flow down from the high citadel of the heavens, so that, sprinkled by them like morning dew and seasonal rain, the Devil—the author of the barrenness of pious minds—may no longer presume to dwell within her with his minions. <note>original: "satellitibus." Literally "satellites," but used in period Latin to mean subordinates, henchmen, or lesser demons serving a leader.</note> Therefore, mitigate all Your anger, and turn away from the wrath of Your indignation. Be merciful and favorable to the sins of this fragile sheep of Your pasture, and abound in mercy so that You may turn away Your wrath. Do not kindle all Your indignation, so that the one made humble may not be turned away in confusion. Lift up Your hand against the pride of these spirits of darkness forever, for those who hate You have boasted. Arise, O Lord God, judge Your cause, for their pride has held them fast; they are covered in their own iniquity and impiety. Their iniquity has come forth as if from fatness <note>original: "quasi ex adipe." A biblical idiom (Psalm 73:7) suggesting that their sin arises from excessive wealth, pride, or a bloated ego.</note>; they have followed the whims of their hearts. They have thought and spoken iniquity; they have spoken from on high. But have they not even set their mouth against heaven? Therefore, O Lord, let their tongue pass away upon the earth, into whose bowels, by Your piety, let them now depart and descend from here. For our mouth shall then be filled with praise, so that we may sing Your glory and Your greatness all the day long. Our tongues shall meditate on Your justice, when those who seek to do us evil have been confounded and put to shame. Thus we will confess to You, O God; we will confess and call upon Your name, saying: Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, who alone does wonders, and blessed be the name of His majesty forever. So be it, so be it. Amen.

I exorcise you, unclean spirits, who by your rash presumption have settled in the body of this creature of God <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "inseditis," meaning "you have settled" or "you inhabit."</note>

<meta>This page continues the formal command to the possessing spirits. The previous page ended with a description of the pride of the demons and a plea for God to arise and judge His cause. # EXORCISMS.

...you who have settled within <note>original: "inseditis," referring to the demons occupying the person's body or soul.</note>, of whatever order or rank you may be, I, a minister of God and servant of our Lord Jesus Christ, [command you] by the indivisible unity and supreme Trinity—in which the Son is ineffably begotten, and who, wishing to consecrate the world by His most holy arrival in the sixth age of the world, was made man of a virgin to redeem humanity, whom He joined by the bond of charity in the unity of faith into one holy Church, His mystical body, of which He is the head: to the extent that it is in no way lawful for you to remain any longer in this creature of God. And if perhaps you boast in a spell <note>original: "maleficio"; this refers to a curse or "evil doing" used by a sorcerer to invite the demon in.</note>, you are bound to dissolve it; and once dissolved, you must cast out its material parts to destroy the whole work, without injury to this creature of God. Indeed, I, by the authority which I exercise, by this present sign of the holy Cross $\dagger$, just as I ordain that you must flee, so I decree that every spell be dissolved, undone, and destroyed.

<margin>What the Church is, and of what kind it is.</margin>
May you be overwhelmed with confusion by the state of the one Catholic and Apostolic Roman Church, which is the gathering of all the faithful living in one faith and visible worship of the true God, united by divine institution for the sake of eternal life. Outside of this Church there is no salvation, but only eternal drowning—just as in the time of Noah, those outside the Ark perished in the flood. In this Church, the good seed perseveres with the tares <note>original: "Zizania," referring to the weeds or "darnel" in the biblical parable of the wheat and the weeds (Matthew 13).</note>; the faithful servants with the negligent; the foolish virgins with the wise; the good fish with the bad; and even the weak, the blind, and the lame, who fill the seats of the wedding feast.

<margin>The Church took its beginning from Adam and Abel.</margin>
May the right hand of those who cast you down be exalted through the origin of the Church. It began radically with Adam in the state of innocence on the sixth day of the work of creation, through his faith, the grace bestowed upon him, and the actual internal sacrifice of his heart (with the intent to perform an external sacrifice in due time if it were necessary). After the creation of Eve, the gathering began; and after the Fall, it obtained the beginning of its visibility in the righteous Abel through grace, faith, and both internal and external sacrifice. It began to multiply successively until our own times, like the stars of heaven and like the sand which is on the 
<margin>Psalm 21.</margin>
shore of the sea. So it was that the holy Prophet said: I will con-

# EXORCISMS

...I will praise you in the great assembly; I will laud you among the mighty people. <note>original: "confitebor tibi..." This is a continuation of Psalm 35:18, where the speaker promises to testify to God’s power in public.</note>

<margin>
The Church is called "One" for three reasons.
</margin>
May God destroy you in the end through the unity of the Church: for it is one, first of all, in the unity of faith, which has remained the same in every state of nature—as it was instituted <note>The state of innocence before the Fall.</note>, as it was destitute <note>The state of sin after the Fall.</note>, and as it was restored <note>The state of grace through Christ.</note>—even if it was witnessed through various and different ceremonies. It was formerly more obscure, yet successively it has always become more evident, as the Apostle says: *One Lord, one faith, one baptism*. Furthermore, it is one in the unity of hope, as he affirms when he says: *You are called in one hope of your calling*. And finally, it is one in the unity of its head, Peter, and his successors visibly presiding over it, according to the prophecy of Joel, who said: *The children of Judah and the children of Israel shall be gathered together, and they shall appoint for themselves one head.* 
<margin>
Joel 1.
</margin>
This head was declared by our Lord to be Peter, when He said: *You shall be called Cephas* <note>original: "Cephas"; a transliteration of the Aramaic word for "Rock" or "Stone," the name Jesus gave to the Apostle Peter.</note>.

<margin>
The Church is Holy.
</margin>
May all your enemies rejoice in your misfortunes through the holiness of the Church. This holiness is not "unshared," since that belongs to God alone, who is holy by His very nature and essence—for it is said:
<margin>
1 Samuel 2.
</margin>
*Who is holy as the Lord?* Rather, it is a "shared" holiness, since the faithful existing in grace are called holy. As the Prophet says of himself: *Guard my soul, for I am holy.* This is not the shared holiness that purges every fault and punishment, for that belongs to the Church Triumphant <note>The Church in Heaven.</note>, but rather it is that which cleanses from both original and actual stain. Regarding this, it was once said to the Romans by the
<margin>
Romans 1.
</margin>
Apostle: *To all who are in Rome, the beloved of God, called to be saints.*

<margin>
The Church is Universal, or Catholic.
</margin>
May all who pass by the way plunder you through the universality of the Church. It is universal in faith, because the faith is universal in the truth of all that is believed, in its permanence through all time, in the duty of all nations to believe according to it, and in the proclamation already made and to be completed throughout all parts of the world. But it is also universal in its sacramental remedy, which cures all spiritual illnesses; in the consistency of all the Churches spread through many provinces; and in the universal presidency of the one Roman Church and the
<margin>
John 10.
</margin>
Great Roman Pontiff <note>The Pope.</note>, so that there may be *one fold and one shepherd*, in whom all the sheep are united, and are committed without distinction to a single Pastor.

<meta>This page continues the exorcism rite by invoking the final "Marks of the Church." Having previously discussed Unity and Holiness, it now focuses on Apostolicity, Infallibility, and the Church's Authority over Scripture to command and shaming demons.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

<margin>The Church is Apostolic.</margin>
Be turned back in shame through the Apostolic nature of the Church, derived from the Holy Apostles of our Lord Jesus Christ. They are the twelve secondary foundations, for no other primary foundation can be laid except that which has been laid, which is Christ Jesus. In confirmation of this truth, John saw the wall of the ecclesiastical city <note>A reference to the New Jerusalem in Revelation 21:14.</note> having twelve foundations, and in them the twelve names of the Apostles and of the Lamb. Therefore, after the ascension of the Lord, they were the first who enlightened the world by word, example, and miracles; for they went forth and preached everywhere, the Lord working with them and confirming the word with the signs that followed. From this source, through their tradition, the sacred ecclesiastical observances have been handed down even to this day.

<margin>The Church cannot err.</margin>
<margin>Luke 22:a</margin>
May the Lord mock you and bring you to nothing through the Church’s inability to wander from the truth and from those things which are of good character. For it cannot be that the Bride whom Christ loved—and for whom He gave Himself up, cleansing her with the washing of water in the word of life, so that He might present the Church to Himself in glory, having no spot or wrinkle or any such thing, but that she might be holy and immaculate—should be permitted to depart from the truth of the faith or the blamelessness of her moral disposition, since the Paraclete <note>original: "Paracletus"; a title for the Holy Spirit, meaning "Advocate" or "Comforter."</note>, the author of all truths, remains with her forever. For this reason, the Lord said to the Apostle: *Peter, I have prayed for you, that your faith may not fail.*

<margin>The nature of the Church's authority.</margin>
May the Lord rebuke you and set a judgment against your face through the authority of the Church, which is so great that it is considered superior to all others. In terms of time, it even precedes the authority of the sacred Scriptures; in the state of the first age, before Scripture existed, the Church existed in different ways in Adam, Eve, and Abel. Even after Christ, the holy messengers of the Gospel began to announce the Gospel throughout the entire world while no Scripture yet existed concerning the incarnate Christ; for the Lord did not say to them, "Go, write," but "Go, preach the Gospel to every creature." It also excels in dignity, because Scripture did not create the Church—even if the Church is proven through Scripture—but rather the Church approved the Scripture.

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Matthew 16.</margin>
May He who exists before the ages humble you through the key of the Church, the power of binding and loosing. By this key, the ecclesiastical judge <note>The "ecclesiastical judge" refers to a priest or bishop acting in a judicial capacity within the Sacrament of Penance.</note>, when he grants remission, opens the way by removing the obstacle to entering heaven, and closes it by withholding such remission. This key is twofold: it is either a key of jurisdiction, through which the one possessing it can exercise authority over another regarding the actions owed to him; or it is a key of order, through which, by the power of the sacramental <term>character</term> <note>In Catholic theology, a "character" is an indelible spiritual mark imprinted on the soul during certain sacraments, such as Ordination.</note>, he is able to either bind or loose. Furthermore, if it is a key of order, it is either a key of knowledge, by which he distinguishes physical leprosy from spiritual leprosy <note>original: "lepram à spiritali lepra"; a metaphor where the priest's ancient role in identifying physical leprosy in Leviticus is compared to the confessor's role in identifying the "leprosy" of sin.</note>; or it is a key of power, by which he pronounces the sentence of absolution or binding. The Lord promised these keys to Peter when He said: *To you I will give the keys of the kingdom of heaven.* <note>original: "Tibi dabo claves regni cœlorum."</note>

<margin>
Whence the
Treasury of the
Church
originates.
</margin>
Carry away your confusion immediately through the Treasury of the Church. Those who have made use of this treasury are made partakers of God’s friendship. There is no need to fear its depletion or diminishment, first because of the infinite and therefore overflowing merit of Jesus Christ, acquired through the passion of His humanity by reason of the identification of the divine nature with the human nature in the unity of the Divine Person; secondly, because of the surpassing sufferings of the Virgin Mary, which she endured even though she was conceived and born innocent and lived always without sin; thirdly, because of the most constant endurance of many torments by the Holy Martyrs for the name of Christ; and finally, because of the <term>supererogation</term> <note>Acts of "supererogation" are good works performed beyond what is required by God's commandments.</note> of the works of all other servants of God.
<margin>Job 7.</margin>
In their number are counted the satisfactory works of Job, who said: *O that my sins, by which I have deserved wrath, and the calamity which I suffer, were weighed in a balance; it would appear heavier than the sands of the sea.* <note>The text cites Job 7, but the quote "Utinam appenderentur..." is actually from Job 6:2–3.</note>

<margin>
What an
Indulgence
is, and its
mention in
scripture.
</margin>
May you wither away quickly like the grass of the field through the support of the Church's indulgence. An indulgence is the distributive application of the surplus penalties <note>original: "pœnalitatum supererogationis"; refers to the "extra" suffering and merits of Christ and the Saints that can be applied to others.</note> of our Lord Jesus Christ and of all the Saints, for the remission of the punishment due for past sins; this application is made with a judgment of discretion by the one who presides over the universal Church. The prophet Isaiah prophesied concerning this, speaking in the person of the Messiah, saying: *The Spirit of the Lord is up-* <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the quote from Isaiah 61:1 on the following page.</note>

# EXORCISMS

...through me, because he has anointed me; he has sent me to preach to the meek, to heal the contrite of heart, and to preach release to the captives. 
<margin>Isaiah 61.</margin>
The Lord our teacher, declaring this in the Synagogue of Nazareth, said that he was sent from the heights of the Father to evangelize the poor, to heal the contrite, and to preach remission to the captives. <note>The exorcist quotes the Messianic prophecy of Isaiah 61:1, which Jesus famously applied to Himself in Luke 4:18. In an exorcism, this serves to remind the demon of Christ’s specific mission to liberate those "held captive" by spiritual forces.</note>

<margin>What and of what kind the value of Indulgences is.</margin>
May God scatter your counsels through the virtue of the Indulgences of the Church, which are most powerful in the internal forum of the Church <note>The "internal forum" usually refers to matters of conscience and the soul, particularly in the Sacrament of Penance.</note>, and in the judgment of God, and in both together. For they absolve the living from the penances enjoined in the forum of conscience; and the same persons from the punishments remaining after Contrition, Confession, and Absolution, even those not enjoined by a penitentiary judge; and likewise they absolve the dead who depart in grace from purgative punishments, when they are applied to them by way of suffrage <note>original: "per modum suffragii"; this refers to prayers or acts offered on behalf of the souls in Purgatory, asking God to apply the merits of the Church to them.</note>. This is not through a claim of personal merit, nor by the command of offerings and prayers, but by way of satisfaction; for while the Church prays for them, she presents to Divine Justice the superabundant punishments <note>original: "supererogativa poenalia"; this refers to the "Treasury of Merit," the infinite spiritual credit earned by Christ and the Saints which exceeds what they "needed" for themselves.</note> of our Lord Jesus Christ and of all the saints for their liberation from punishments. Thus, in an orderly way, she does not remit the satisfaction for the punishment, but rather the obligation to the punishment.

<margin>The Church shall endure until the end of the age.</margin>
May you be made like a lost vessel through the permanence of the Church, because of which she shall draw the perpetuity of her own eternity along with the world, since God has founded her 
<margin>Psalm 147.</margin>
forever; for which reason her priesthood is also said to be eternal, 
<margin>Psalm 110.</margin>
according to the order of Melchizedek <note>Melchizedek was a mysterious priest-king in Genesis; in Christian theology, he is a "type" or precursor to Christ, representing a priesthood that is eternal and superior to the old Law.</note>. Therefore, the 
<margin>Luke 1.</margin>
Angel Gabriel, when speaking to Mary regarding the seat of the Ecclesiastical kingdom, said that the Lord God would give to her son, our Lord Jesus Christ, 
<margin>Matthew (last chapter).</margin>
the seat of David his father, and he shall reign in the house of Jacob forever. 
<margin>Matthew 16.</margin>
For this reason, the Savior said: *I will be with you even unto the consummation of the age* <note>original: "ero vobiscum usque ad consummationem seculi."</note>, so that I may confirm the Church with grace, nourish her with the Eucharist, and finally protect her from the enemy, nor shall the gates of hell prevail against her.

May those who hate you hold dominion over you through the primacy of Peter, and of his successors in the Church, given to him first by our Lord Jesus Christ according to the fullness...

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Primacy in the Church belongs to Peter and his successors.</margin>
> ...of the promise of power, with Him saying: "You are Peter, and upon this rock I will build my Church, and the gates of hell shall not prevail against it; and I will give to you the keys of the kingdom of heaven." <note>original: "Tu es Petrus..."; from Matthew 16:18–19. This "Power of the Keys" is the theological foundation for the exorcist's authority to command and expel demons.</note> This was truly given later when, in the threefold questioning, He had said: "Simon, son of John, do you love me? More than these? Feed my lambs; feed my sheep." <note>John 21:15–17. This passage establishes the Petrine authority—the Pope as the "Supreme Pastor"—over the entire Church. The exorcist uses this to declare the demon an outsider to Christ's flock.</note> From this, it is easily discerned that those whom the pride of insolence keeps from being subject to that supreme pastor are not of the sheepfold of Christ.

<margin>Human laws bind [one] to their observance.</margin>
> May the moments of your inhabitation here vanish like smoke, and decline like a shadow through the due observance of human laws in the Church of God. Those who despise these laws incur the stain of crime; for human law flows from natural law, and natural law is derived from the divine. <margin>Proverbs 8.</margin> Thus, with God as the author, kings reign, and princes command, and the makers of laws decree what is just. <note>Proverbs 8:15. The exorcist argues that because human law is part of a divine hierarchy, the demon is legally bound to obey the commands of the Church.</note> Therefore, every soul ought to be subject to the higher powers in the observance of laws, for there is no power except from God; and those powers that exist are ordained by God. Therefore, whoever resists the power, resists the ordinance of God, and those who resist bring damnation upon themselves. <note>Romans 13:1–2. This is a common rhetorical move in exorcism: framing the demon's possession as an illegal "occupation" that violates the cosmic and legal order established by God.</note>

<margin>Canonical traditions and laws must be observed.</margin>
> May your enemies trouble you through all the pious canonical traditions, which lead by ruling rightly, while they provide the form for living correctly and correct what is crooked. For they are ordained for the spiritual good and for a peaceful way of Church life; indeed, led by this pious intention, the Apostles left such traditions to be observed by the faithful. <margin>2 Thessalonians 2.</margin> From this, the Apostle warned the Thessalonians to hold the traditions they had learned, whether by letter or by word of mouth. <note>2 Thessalonians 2:15. This emphasizes that the Church's oral and written traditions are just as authoritative as the Bible itself in the battle against evil.</note> And as he traveled through Syria confirming the Church and passed through cities, he delivered the precepts and dogmas of the Apostles and the Elders, most insistently commanding that they be kept.

<margin>Holy Scripture is of the greatest authority.</margin>
> May you be struck down like withering grass by the authority of Holy Scripture, in which such faith must always be placed in all matters that no fear ever terrifies one into the opposite view. For what is more certain than Scripture? For holy men of God spoke as the Holy Spirit breathed upon them; <note>2 Peter 1:21.</note> the Holy Spirit, moreover...

# EXORCISMS

Since truth, by its very essence, cannot possibly bring forth lies; therefore, the Lord Jesus Christ—when he had confirmed his gospel with miracles and noticed that false prophets would give signs and wonders in the eyes of men, so that even the elect, if possible, might be led into error—turned to the firmness of scripture, saying: "Search the scriptures, because it is they which bear witness of me." <note>original: "Scrutamini scripturas..."; from John 5:39. The author argues that while demons can fake miracles, they cannot stand against the absolute truth of the Bible.</note>

<margin>What faith is, and what its object and necessity are. Hebrews 11.</margin>
May the Almighty send upon you the wrath of His indignation—indignation, and wrath, and tribulation—and the sending of evil angels <note>This refers to Psalm 78:49, where God punishes the wicked; the exorcist is calling down divine punishment upon the demon.</note> through the faith of the Church, which is the foundation and substance of things hoped for, and the evidence of things not seen; <note>original: "substantia sperandarum rerum..."; This is the classic definition of Faith from Hebrews 11:1.</note> the seat of which is the intellect, being an assent without fear, as is the nature of faith; whose object is the first divine truth revealing, or the divine thing itself revealed, which is imperceptible to the senses during this earthly life, even if faith comes from hearing, and hearing through the word of Christ; and whose necessity for salvation is so great that the Apostle said it is impossible for anyone to please God without it. <note>Hebrews 11:6. The exorcist is telling the demon that because it lacks faith, it is entirely excluded from the protection of God.</note>

<margin>The articles of faith: what they are and of what kind.</margin>
May your ways become dark and slippery, and may the angel of the Lord pursue you through the twelve articles of the Faith of the Church <note>The "Twelve Articles" refer to the Apostles' Creed, which was traditionally believed to have been composed by the twelve apostles.</note>, of which six pertain to the divinity, and just as many to the humanity of Christ; Therefore, first, we most truly confess one God, triune, creator, justifier, restorer to life, and glorifier; just as we then most constantly profess with a believing heart and an open mouth the incarnation of Christ, His death, descent into hell, resurrection, ascension, and His coming for judgment; For the Savior wonderfully encompassed all these things regarding both natures, saying:
<margin>John 17.</margin>
"This is eternal life, that they may know you, the only true God, and Jesus Christ, whom you have sent." <note>John 17:3.</note>

<margin>
The Canonization of Saints, which pertains to the Supreme Pontiff: why the rite is performed.
</margin>
May you be made like dust before the face of the wind, and let the angel of the Lord drive you away through the profession of the faith of the Church, which is performed especially in the canonization of Saints, which belongs only to the supreme pontiff; for just as it belongs to God alone and our Lord Jesus Christ to sanctify, so it ought to belong only to the Vicar of Christ <note>The Pope.</note> on earth [to recognize] the holiness of men...

# EXORCISMS

...by observing the due rites and solemnities, to declare; and thus both to approve their holiness and propose it for veneration by the Christian people, and by their benefit to confirm the faith of the whole Church, so that in this way he may fulfill the saying of the Savior, who says:

<margin>Luke 22.</margin>

<margin>
Justification is threefold.
Proverbs 1.
</margin>
May the Lord repay you abundantly, you who act in pride, through **Justification**, <note>In this context, Justification is the process by which a soul is moved from a state of sin to a state of grace. The exorcist is "repaying" the demon's pride with the weight of divine law.</note> by which a rational creature can be justified in various ways: either through the simple outpouring of God’s grace without a prior preparation of the will—as with all the Angels, and some men, through the Lord's free precedence; or through the bestowing of grace with the application of the **Sacrament of Baptism**, without the cooperation of the choice of those being sanctified—as with human infants before the use of reason; or through the remission of guilt and the infusion of grace on God’s part, combined with the due preparation of the mind on the part of those to be reconciled—as commonly happens in adult men; for the heart prepares its way, but it is the Lord’s to direct its steps. <note>original: "Cor enim disponit viam suam..."; a paraphrase of Proverbs 16:9.</note>

<margin>Special grace is of two kinds.</margin>
May your iniquities be visited with a rod, and your sins with lashes, <note>A ritual curse directed at the demon, framing the theological concept of Grace as a weapon of punishment.</note> through **special grace**, by which God moves a person toward justification: either when He leads them to consider the enormity of sin and the bitterness of the due punishment—yet before the infusion of sanctifying grace <note><term>sanctifying grace</term> (Latin: *gratia gratum faciens*): the supernatural gift that makes the soul holy and pleasing to God.</note>—by disposing them gradually and gently, when the **Conscience** <note><term>Synderesis</term>: a technical term in medieval theology referring to the innate "spark" of conscience that recognizes basic moral principles.</note> pricks them, and by the light of faith, the freedom of the will, and longing effort, a person is able (even if imperfectly) to move toward the good; or when He gives grace to a person immediately and suddenly, without preparation, beyond the natural mode, and leads them at once to perfect good; so that in all things it may appear that we are not sufficient to think anything perfectly of ourselves as if from ourselves, but our sufficiency comes from God.

May you be caught in your pride through all the causes concurring for the justification of the ungodly: through the disposing causes—Faith, Hope, Charity, and Penance, by which...

# EXORCISMS

<margin>What and how many the causes of justification are.</margin>
...we acquire the beginning, progress, and perfection toward the good: through the **Principal cause**, <note>In scholastic philosophy, the 'Principal cause' is the primary agent—in this case, God.</note> God the best and greatest, for He is the one who justifies the ungodly; through the **Meritorious cause**, our Lord Jesus Christ, for He earned grace for us and opened the way to glory; through the **Excellent Ministerial cause**, the humanity of our Lord Jesus Christ, which, united to the divinity, sufficiently bestowed spiritual gifts upon us; through the **Simple Ministerial cause**, the Priest of the Lord, who has the authority of absolving and applying the sacred medicines; through the **Instrumental cause**, <note>The 'Instrument' is the tool used by the agent; here, the Sacraments are the tools of God’s grace.</note> the Holy Sacraments, which signify, contain, and confer grace; through the **Formal cause**, sanctifying grace, <note>original: "gratiam gratum facientem"; this is the specific type of grace that makes a soul pleasing to God.</note> by which, having turned away from evil, we are converted toward the good; and through the **Final cause**, eternal life, which we hope to possess and enjoy forever.

<margin>The manifold graces of God have many effects.</margin>
May your seat be shattered upon the earth through **Grace**, by which God loves man in various ways: generally and specially; by freely giving the *word of wisdom*, so that things pertaining to the divine may be manifested deeply through supreme causes; the *word of knowledge*, so that the things of God may be made known through created things; the *word of faith*, so that belief may be persuaded; the *grace of healing*, so that faith may be more easily attained through the benefit of health; the *working of miracles*, so that some great external work may be performed; *prophecy*, so that divinely known things may be revealed to others; the *discernment of spirits*, so that with a distinction made, the truth may be shown; the *variety of tongues*, so that the truth may be commended to diverse nations; the *interpretation of words*, so that the difficulties of Scripture may be untied; and *administration*, so that the Ecclesiastical Hierarchy may be governed. But also finally by making one pleasing; beginning in this Life, and being perfected in the Homeland. <note>original: "in Via... in Patria"; the "Way" refers to our journey on earth, while the "Homeland" refers to the soul's true home in Heaven.</note>

<margin>The human will is always free.</margin>
May the Lord be great to you, and terrible over all those who are around Him, through the **freedom of the human will**; because of which, in the Homeland, the will is free concerning the Highest Good by the *freedom of complacency*; just as in the Way, concerning any object, while it wills this or that, or its opposite, it possesses the *freedom of contrariety*; or in respect to its own act, while it is able either to will or not to will, it possesses the *freedom of contradiction*; and as for the order of existing or apparent good, it possesses the *freedom of arbitration*...

<summary>This page lists the various theological "causes" of justification (Principal, Meritorious, etc.) and the charismatic gifts of the Holy Spirit used to combat the demon. It concludes by defining the different types of human free will as a testament to God's power over the fallen spirits.</summary>
<keywords>justification, sanctifying grace, sacraments, free will, discernment of spirits, ecclesiastical hierarchy, scholasticism, Christology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific theological arguments used to bind the demonic entity. Having previously established the "causes" of salvation, the text now focuses on the necessity of good works and the spiritual power found in the hierarchy of veneration—distinguishing between the honor given to saints and the worship due to God.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

<margin>Genesis 4</margin>
...entirely regarding exercise, whether it suspends its own action or does not; both before the sin of Adam and after his fall; with the Lord saying to Cain, already a willful fratricide: "Your desire shall be under you, and you shall rule over it." <note>original: "Supter te erit appetitus tuus, & tu dominaberis illius." This refers to the Catholic doctrine of free will, asserting that even after the Fall, humans (and by extension, the target of the exorcism) have the capacity to choose to master their sinful impulses.</note>

<margin>Good works are effective for various ends.</margin>
May God pour His wrath upon you, and may the fury of His anger seize you through the good works of all Christians performed in charity. These works, because they please God, are especially necessary for salvation; both because they bring life to faith, which without works is dead; and because of the good example shown to neighbors through their practice, 
<margin>Romans 2</margin> 
so that they may glorify the Father who is in heaven; and also because of the eternal reward expected from God, who renders to each according to his works: to those indeed who, by patience in good work, seek glory, honor, and incorruption—eternal life.

<margin>The intercession of the Saints must be requested.</margin>
May our protector, the Lord, scatter you by His power and cast you down through the fitting invocation of all the saints by men. As the household of God, may they worthily implore divine mercy for us in our needs through their intervening merits, just as they were accustomed to do before the end of their lives—as when Abraham prayed for the health of Abimelech—and after death, as Jeremiah did for the people of Israel and the whole holy city. 
<margin>Exodus 32; Psalm 148</margin> 
For it is right to invoke them thus, as when Moses said to the Lord: "Remember Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob"; so that we may praise the Lord in His saints, whom we know He honors as they minister to Him.

<margin>The Saints are to be venerated.</margin>
May you come to nothing like running water through the laudable adoration of the saints known as **Dulia**, <term>Dulia: From the Greek word for "service," this is the honor or veneration given to saints and angels as servants of God.</term> by which the Saints are commonly honored in recognition of their specific dignity; or **Hyperdulia**, <term>Hyperdulia: A superior level of veneration reserved specifically for the Virgin Mary, acknowledging her unique role above all other created beings.</term> by which those saints who stand preeminent above the common state are venerated, not only by an internal act, but also by an external one—just as Abigail adored David, falling prostrate before him and at his feet; and as the prophet Nathan did, bowing to the ground in reverence to him. Meanwhile, honor and glory, the worship of **Latria**, <term>Latria: The highest form of worship, which Catholic theology teaches is strictly reserved for the Holy Trinity alone.</term> is reserved for the immortal and invisible King of ages, the only God.

<meta>catchword: Descending</meta>

<summary>This page of the exorcism ritual uses the doctrines of good works, the intercession of saints, and the specific degrees of religious veneration (Dulia, Hyperdulia, and Latria) to command and weaken the demon. It reinforces the power of the Church's spiritual hierarchy and the efficacy of the saints' prayers in the struggle against evil.</summary>

--- Page 161 ---
<meta>This page continues the exorcist’s legalistic and theological assault on the demonic presence, moving from the necessity of "good works" (on the previous page) to the specific spiritual power contained in religious icons and the historical precedents of biblical villains who were defeated by God.</meta>

# EXORCISMS

<margin>How the images of the Saints are to be venerated.</margin>
May you at last descend living into hell through the due veneration of venerable images in the Church of God. By this act, we honor those who represent the holy ones, directing our reverence through the image toward the original subject <note>original: "prototypum"</note>, honoring the image just as we honor the one represented. For the children of Israel were bound by the Lord's command to look reverently upon the image of the bronze serpent—which was fashioned and hung upon a wooden pole—so that they would not perish when struck by living serpents in the desert. Nevertheless, their reverence did not end with the object itself, nor were they healed by the object, but rather by Christ, who was signified by being lifted upon the wood of the cross. This was a sign of a healing remedy acquired through the merit of His passion for all people, who have been struck in various ways by you—the ancient serpents. Thus, while we honor the images of the Saints, we simultaneously venerate those depicted, always and everywhere. But now, and here especially, we do so so that through their intercessions they may hasten to our aid and, strengthened by the weapons of God's favor, fight against you with a strong hand and a powerful arm. 

<margin>Psalm 74</margin> 
Will you be able to resist all of them? Can you resist God, whom we believe is now made favorable to us through their intercession? Do not—do not lift up your horn on high; do not speak iniquity against God, but be humbled, trembling under the powerful hand of God, nor let any power to act shamelessly remain within you. Do you still presume to stand against God? Flee from here! Give place to God! Leave behind all your sorceries <term>maleficialia: works of harmful magic or demonic influence</term>. For by the authority I exercise, I definitively decree that the sign of the holy cross † shall intervene between you and these things, so that your strengths may be entirely taken away and your evil spells reduced to nothing. 

<margin>The Devil is compared to various figures.</margin> 
Do not, therefore, be rebels! Do not, like Antiochus, contaminate the vessels of God; do not, like Joab, lay snares; do not, like Ahab, profane the vineyard of God; do not, like Apollonius, plunder the spoils of the temple of the Lord; and do not, like Holofernes, thirst for human blood. <note>The exorcist here lists notorious biblical villains: Antiochus IV (who desecrated the Temple), Joab (David’s treacherous general), Ahab (the idolatrous king), Apollonius (a looter of the treasury), and Holofernes (the general beheaded by Judith). By linking the demon to these figures, the exorcist "names" the spirit's crimes and reminds it of its inevitable defeat.</note> But go to the places appointed for you, prepared by God, where you may harm no one. Instead, by the just judgment of God, who sits upon the throne of His equity, may all things cause you harm for ages unto ages.

K

# EXORCISMS

# THE EIGHTH EXORCISM
## Which is drawn from the mysteries of the Most Holy Trinity and the unity of God.

¶ *While the Exorcist stands before the altar, let him say the following Psalm.*

<margin>Psalm 110 <note>original: "Psal. 109." This follows the Vulgate numbering; in most modern Bibles, this is Psalm 110.</note></margin>
The Lord said to my Lord: Sit at my right hand, until I make your enemies a footstool for your feet. The Lord will send the rod of your power out of Zion: rule in the midst of your enemies. With you is the dominion in the day of your power, in the brightness of the saints: from the womb before the morning star <note>original: "ante Luciferum." In this context, it refers to the dawn or the morning star as a symbol of Christ’s eternal generation, not the fallen angel.</note> I have begotten you. The Lord has sworn, and he will not repent: You are a priest forever, according to the order of Melchizedek <note>A mysterious king and priest from the Old Testament, seen here as a precursor to Christ.</note>. The Lord is at your right hand: he has crushed kings in the day of his wrath. He shall judge among the nations, he shall fill them with ruins: he shall shatter heads in the land of many. He drank from the torrent on the way: therefore he has lifted up his head. Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, etc.

¶ *Turning toward the possessed person, let the Exorcist say the following reading.*

->A Reading from the Acts of the Apostles.<-

<margin>Acts 8.</margin>
Philip, going down to the city of Samaria, preached Christ to them. And the crowds with one accord gave heed to the things spoken by Philip, hearing and seeing the signs which he performed. For many of those who had unclean spirits, crying out with a loud voice, went out.

¶ *With his hand placed upon the crown of the possessed person’s head, let the Exorcist say the following from the Holy Gospel.*

<margin>Mark 7.</margin>
Jesus went away into the borders of Tyre and Sidon, and entering into a house, he wanted no one to know it, but he could not remain hidden. For a woman, as soon as she heard of him, whose daughter had

<note>This is the conclusion of the Gospel according to Mark, chapter 7.</note> ...who had an unclean spirit, entered. And she fell at his feet. For the woman was a Gentile, a Syrophoenician by race. And she begged him to cast the demon out of her daughter. But he said to her: “Let the children first be fed; for it is not good to take the children’s bread and throw it to the dogs.” But she answered and said to him: “Yes, Lord; for even the puppies under the table eat from the children’s crumbs.” And he said to her: “Because of this word, go; the demon has gone out of your daughter.” And when she had gone home, she found the girl lying upon the bed, and the demon had gone out.

## P R A Y E R .

<margin>How the two divine persons are brought forth.</margin> <note>The marginal note refers to the theological doctrine of "procession," explaining how the Son is "generated" and the Spirit "breathed forth" from the Father.</note>
O most noble divine nature of infinite majesty, the single cause of all beings, and the most simple essence of being: yet not excluding the plurality of three persons. Since you are most powerful, most wise, and most good: you are able, you know, and you will from eternity that the Father gives the fullness of his majesty to another, generating a Son equal to himself, who with the Father breathes forth the Spirit of love, a Maker and creator with the same Father and Son of all things visible and invisible. I beg you, lead not this your creation into diabolical temptation; but just as you have filled it until now with the wormwood of bitterness, so thereafter deign to sprinkle it with the dew of your mercy. And just as you performed wonders in the land of Ham <note>original: "terra Cham." A biblical name for Egypt, referring to the descendants of Noah's son Ham.</note>: the striking down in Egypt, from man even unto beast, and terrifying things in the Red Sea: so expel these demons cursed by you, and by a terrible confusion cast them down into the snare of destruction, so that a storm of water might submerge them, and the deep swallow them up, and the pit close its mouth over them. <note>This imagery draws from Psalm 69:15, using the metaphor of drowning to describe the demon's expulsion into the abyss.</note> Let them dread your wonderful name: let them flee and withdraw far away, so that we, the sheep of your pasture, together with this your creature liberated from diabolical tyranny, may exult in your holy name and rejoice to you in psalms, saying: Blessed be the Lord, who has not given us up as a prey to their teeth: but our soul has been rescued from the snare

# EXORCISMS

...the snare of the hunters is broken, and we have been delivered. <note>A reference to Psalm 124:7, commonly used in the liturgy to celebrate deliverance from evil.</note> Through our Lord Jesus Christ, who by his own power trampled upon the proud and rebellious Devil, and who lives and reigns with the same Father and the Holy Spirit in the unity of the ineffable majesty of his essence, forever and ever. Amen.

->¶ *Here the Exorcist shall sit and complete the following.*<-

### EXORCISM

Hear, O asps of hell, <note>original: "Aspides tartarei." The "asp" or cobra was a common biblical metaphor for the deaf and stubborn nature of evil spirits.</note> of whom it is said: *Their bread shall be turned into the gall of asps.* <note>A reference to Job 20:14, suggesting that the "food" or pleasure the demon finds in possession will turn into poison.</note> I exorcise you again and again, of whatever order or kind you may be: Fiery, Airy, Earthly, Watery, and Subterranean, <note>This reflects the Renaissance belief that demons were categorized by the four elements, plus those who lived deep underground.</note> whether of the greater or the lesser sort; so that, making no delay, you may immediately depart from this creature of God—whom you rashly presume to invade—fleeing in haste. Moreover, setting aside every deception, you must extinguish and utterly scatter every kind of witchcraft, and most completely extract any material spells that may exist, <margin>What the Being of God is like. Psalm 89.</margin> by that most noble Being, <note>original: "Esse." This refers to God’s existence as pure, unchanging being.</note> outside of whom nothing exists. For nothing concerning him is past or future, but the whole remains simultaneously present, which belongs to God alone; as David, led by divine power, once sang: *Before the mountains were brought forth, or the earth and the world were formed, from everlasting to everlasting, you are God.* <note>Psalm 90:2 (Vulgate 89:2).</note>

<margin>What the Unity of God is like.</margin> I exorcise you by the most simple unity of God, which contains nothing within itself except itself; and which, retaining the likeness of a created unity, just as that unity proceeds from no descent or consideration, and all plurality flows successively from it, <margin>Ephesians 4.</margin> so God is from no one, but all things are from him. Of this divine unity the Apostle <note>Traditionally referring to St. Paul.</note> makes mention, saying: *One Lord, one faith, one baptism.* <note>Ephesians 4:5.</note>

<margin>There is one God, the ruler of all. Ephesians 4.</margin> I exorcise you by the truth of only one principle, <note>original: "principium." Meaning the ultimate source or beginning of all things.</note> the creator and ruler of the entire universe, as the Apostle declares, saying: *One God and Father of all, who is over all.* <note>Ephesians 4:6.</note> Since a plurality of rulers is not good, therefore just as one God alone created the heaven and the earth in the beginning, so has he always had care for all things.

<meta>catchword: Hear-</meta>

<summary>The exorcist commands demons of all elemental types (fire, air, earth, water, and subterranean) to depart and destroy their spells, invoking the eternal nature and absolute unity of God as the source of his authority.</summary>
